## Get-PWWorkAreaForDocument


NAME
    Get-PWWorkAreaForDocument
    
SYNOPSIS
    Returns the containing Work Area for the specified document.
    
    
SYNTAX
    Get-PWWorkAreaForDocument [-InputDocument] <ProjectWiseDocument> 
    [<CommonParameters>]
    
    
DESCRIPTION
    The Get-PWWorkAreaForDocument cmdlet retrieves the Work Area (Rich 
    Project) that contains the specified ProjectWise document.
    
    A Work Area is a special folder type in ProjectWise that provides 
    additional project management capabilities. This cmdlet is useful for 
    determining which Work Area a document belongs to, which can be helpful 
    for navigation, reporting, or workflow automation.
    
    If the document is not located within a Work Area, a warning message is 
    displayed indicating that no Work Area was found for the document.
    

PARAMETERS
    -InputDocument <ProjectWiseDocument>
        Specifies the ProjectWise document for which to retrieve the 
        containing Work Area. This parameter accepts a ProjectWiseDocument 
        object and supports pipeline input.
        
        Required?                    true
        Position?                    0
        Default value                None
        Accept pipeline input?       True (ByValue)
        Accept wildcard characters?  false
        
    <CommonParameters>
        This cmdlet supports the common parameters: Verbose, Debug,
        ErrorAction, ErrorVariable, WarningAction, WarningVariable,
        OutBuffer, PipelineVariable, and OutVariable. For more information, 
    see 
        about_CommonParameters 
    (https:/go.microsoft.com/fwlink/?LinkID=113216). 
    
INPUTS
    PWPS_DAB.CommonTypes+ProjectWiseDocument
        A ProjectWiseDocument object representing the document for which to 
        find the containing Work Area. This object can be obtained from 
        cmdlets such as Get-PWDocumentsByGUIDs.
    
    
OUTPUTS
    PWPS_DAB.CommonTypes+ProjectWiseFolder
        A ProjectWiseFolder object representing the Work Area (Rich Project) 
        that contains the specified document. The output object includes 
        properties such as:
        
        - Name : The name of the Work Area. - Description : The description 
        of the Work Area. - FullPath : The full path to the Work Area in the 
        ProjectWise folder hierarchy. - ProjectID : The unique identifier of 
        the Work Area. - ProjectURN : The URN of the Work Area. - 
        IsRichProject : Indicates whether the folder is a Work Area (always 
        True for this cmdlet's output).
    
    
NOTES
    
    
        - This cmdlet requires an active connection to a ProjectWise 
        datasource.
        
        - If the document is not located within a Work Area, a warning is 
        displayed and no output is returned.
        
        - The cmdlet processes documents one at a time when receiving 
        pipeline input, returning a Work Area object for each document that 
        is within a Work Area.
    
    -------------------------- EXAMPLE 1 --------------------------
    
    Get-PWDocumentsByGUIDs -DocumentGUIDs 
    'ca4805a1-e44d-4389-99fa-c14ac44ba33f' | Get-PWWorkAreaForDocument
    
    Retrieves a document by its GUID and pipes it to 
    Get-PWWorkAreaForDocument to return the containing Work Area.
    -------------------------- EXAMPLE 2 --------------------------
    
    $document = Get-PWDocumentsByGUIDs -DocumentGUIDs 
    'ca4805a1-e44d-4389-99fa-c14ac44ba33f'
    $workArea = Get-PWWorkAreaForDocument -InputDocument $document
    
    Retrieves a document by its GUID and stores it in a variable. Then 
    retrieves the containing Work Area using the -InputDocument parameter 
    and stores it in the $workArea variable for further use.
    -------------------------- EXAMPLE 3 --------------------------
    
    $documents = Get-PWDocumentsByGUIDs -DocumentGUIDs 
    'ca4805a1-e44d-4389-99fa-c14ac44ba33f', 
    'be54669c-6411-4a34-a0c5-186e28b7a2b3'
    $documents | Get-PWWorkAreaForDocument
    
    Retrieves multiple documents by their GUIDs and pipes them to 
    Get-PWWorkAreaForDocument. The cmdlet processes each document and 
    returns the containing Work Area for each one.
    -------------------------- EXAMPLE 4 --------------------------
    
    $document = Get-PWDocumentsByGUIDs -DocumentGUIDs 
    'ca4805a1-e44d-4389-99fa-c14ac44ba33f'
    $workArea = $document | Get-PWWorkAreaForDocument
    $workArea.Name
    $workArea.FullPath
    
    Retrieves a document, gets its containing Work Area, and then accesses 
    specific properties of the Work Area such as Name and FullPath.
    
RELATED LINKS





--

## Get-PWWorkAreaForFolder


NAME
    Get-PWWorkAreaForFolder
    
SYNOPSIS
    Returns the containing Work Area for a specified folder.
    
    
SYNTAX
    Get-PWWorkAreaForFolder [-InputFolder] <ProjectWiseFolder> 
    [<CommonParameters>]
    
    
DESCRIPTION
    The Get-PWWorkAreaForFolder cmdlet retrieves the Work Area that contains 
    the specified input folder. A Work Area is a special folder type in 
    ProjectWise that serves as a container for organizing project-related 
    documents and subfolders.
    
    If the input folder is already a Work Area, the cmdlet returns that same 
    folder. If the folder is not contained within any Work Area, a warning 
    message is displayed.
    

PARAMETERS
    -InputFolder <ProjectWiseFolder>
        Specifies the ProjectWise folder for which to retrieve the 
        containing Work Area. This parameter accepts a ProjectWiseFolder 
        object and supports pipeline input.
        
        Required?                    true
        Position?                    0
        Default value                None
        Accept pipeline input?       True (ByValue)
        Accept wildcard characters?  false
        
    <CommonParameters>
        This cmdlet supports the common parameters: Verbose, Debug,
        ErrorAction, ErrorVariable, WarningAction, WarningVariable,
        OutBuffer, PipelineVariable, and OutVariable. For more information, 
    see 
        about_CommonParameters 
    (https:/go.microsoft.com/fwlink/?LinkID=113216). 
    
INPUTS
    PWPS_DAB.CommonTypes+ProjectWiseFolder
        A ProjectWiseFolder object representing the folder for which to find 
        the containing Work Area.
    
    
OUTPUTS
    PWPS_DAB.CommonTypes+ProjectWiseFolder
        Returns a ProjectWiseFolder object representing the Work Area. The 
        returned object includes the following key properties:
        
        - ProjectID : The unique identifier of the Work Area. - Name : The 
        name of the Work Area. - Description : The description of the Work 
        Area. - FullPath : The full path of the Work Area in the folder 
        hierarchy. - IsRichProject : Boolean indicating this is a Work Area 
        (always True for returned objects). - Code : The folder code. - 
        Environment : The environment assigned to the Work Area. - Workflow 
        : The workflow assigned to the Work Area. - Storage : The storage 
        area name. - ProjectURN : The URN of the Work Area. - ProjectGUID : 
        The GUID of the Work Area. - CreateDateTime : The date and time the 
        Work Area was created. - UpdateDateTime : The date and time the Work 
        Area was last updated. - ConnectedProjectId : The GUID of the 
        connected project (if applicable).
    
    
NOTES
    
    
        - If the input folder is already a Work Area, the cmdlet returns 
        that same folder.
        
        - If the input folder is not contained within any Work Area, the 
        cmdlet displays a warning message and returns no output.
        
        - The cmdlet only processes one folder at a time from the pipeline.
    
    -------------------------- EXAMPLE 1 --------------------------
    
    Get-PWFolders -FolderPath 'My Folder\Subfolder' | Get-PWWorkAreaForFolder
    
    Returns the Work Area containing the folder located at 'My 
    Folder\Subfolder' using pipeline input.
    -------------------------- EXAMPLE 2 --------------------------
    
    $folder = Get-PWFolders -FolderPath 'Projects\Design\Drawings'
    Get-PWWorkAreaForFolder -InputFolder $folder
    
    Returns the Work Area containing the specified folder using the 
    InputFolder parameter.
    -------------------------- EXAMPLE 3 --------------------------
    
    Get-PWFolderByURN 
    'pw://server.bentley.com:datasource/Documents/P{guid}/' | 
    Get-PWWorkAreaForFolder
    
    Returns the Work Area for a folder retrieved by its URN.
    -------------------------- EXAMPLE 4 --------------------------
    
    $workArea = Get-PWFolders -FolderPath 'My Work Area' | 
    Get-PWWorkAreaForFolder
    $workArea.Name
    $workArea.FullPath
    $workArea.IsRichProject
    
    Retrieves the Work Area and accesses its properties such as Name, 
    FullPath, and IsRichProject.
    -------------------------- EXAMPLE 5 --------------------------
    
    Get-PWFolders -FolderPath 'Standalone Folder' | Get-PWWorkAreaForFolder
    # WARNING: Work Area not found for 'Standalone Folder'
    
    Demonstrates the warning message displayed when a folder is not 
    contained within any Work Area.
    
RELATED LINKS





--

## Get-PWWorkAreaProperties


NAME
    Get-PWWorkAreaProperties
    
SYNOPSIS
    Gets the list of available work area properties (attributes) from the 
    datasource.
    
    
SYNTAX
    Get-PWWorkAreaProperties [[-ProjectType] <String>] [<CommonParameters>]
    
    
DESCRIPTION
    The `Get-PWWorkAreaProperties` cmdlet retrieves work area properties 
    (attributes) defined in ProjectWise. Work area properties are custom 
    attributes associated with different work area types (project types) in 
    the datasource.
    
    - When a ProjectType is specified, the cmdlet returns only the property 
    names (as strings) for that specific work area type. - When no 
    ProjectType is specified, the cmdlet returns all properties across all 
    work area types. Each result is an object containing both the 
    `ProjectType` and `PropertyName`.
    
    This cmdlet is useful for discovering available properties before 
    filtering work areas using `Get-PWWorkAreas` with the 
    `-ProjectProperties` parameter. Property names are case-sensitive when 
    used with `Get-PWWorkAreas`.
    

PARAMETERS
    -ProjectType <String>
        Specifies the work area project type for which to retrieve 
        properties. If not specified, properties for all project types are 
        returned.
        
        Required?                    false
        Position?                    0
        Default value                None
        Accept pipeline input?       True (ByValue)
        Accept wildcard characters?  false
        
    <CommonParameters>
        This cmdlet supports the common parameters: Verbose, Debug,
        ErrorAction, ErrorVariable, WarningAction, WarningVariable,
        OutBuffer, PipelineVariable, and OutVariable. For more information, 
    see 
        about_CommonParameters 
    (https:/go.microsoft.com/fwlink/?LinkID=113216). 
    
INPUTS
    System.String
        You can pipe a project type name to this cmdlet.
    
    
OUTPUTS
    System.String[]
        When the `-ProjectType` parameter is specified, the cmdlet returns 
        an array of property names as strings.
    
    System.Management.Automation.PSObject[]
        When no `-ProjectType` is specified, the cmdlet returns an array of 
        objects with the following properties:
        
        - ProjectType : The name of the work area type. - PropertyName : The 
        name of the property associated with that work area type.
    
    
NOTES
    
    
        - This cmdlet requires an active connection to a ProjectWise 
        datasource.
        
        - The properties returned are the custom attributes defined for work 
        area types in the ProjectWise environment.
        
        - Property names are case-sensitive when used with the 
        `Get-PWWorkAreas -ProjectProperties` parameter.
        
        - Use this cmdlet to discover available properties before filtering 
        or querying work areas with `Get-PWWorkAreas`.
    
    Example 1: Get all work area properties across all project types
    
    PS C:\> Get-PWWorkAreaProperties
    
    Returns all work area properties from all project types in the 
    datasource. Each result includes both `ProjectType` and `PropertyName` 
    properties.
    ---- Example 2: Get properties for a specific project type ----
    
    PS C:\> Get-PWWorkAreaProperties -ProjectType "General"
    
    Returns only the property names associated with the "General" work area 
    type as an array of strings.
    -- Example 3: Use pipeline input to specify the project type --
    
    PS C:\> "General" | Get-PWWorkAreaProperties
    
    Uses pipeline input to retrieve property names for the "General" work 
    area type.
    --- Example 4: Store properties in a variable for later use ---
    
    PS C:\> $properties = Get-PWWorkAreaProperties -ProjectType "General"
    PS C:\> $properties
    
    Retrieves property names for the "General" project type and stores them 
    in a variable for reference.
    --- Example 5: Store all properties with their project types ---
    
    PS C:\> $allProperties = Get-PWWorkAreaProperties
    PS C:\> $allProperties
    
    Retrieves all properties across all project types and stores them in a 
    variable. Each item contains `ProjectType` and `PropertyName`.
    ---- Example 6: Discover properties then filter work areas ----
    
    PS C:\> Get-PWWorkAreaProperties -ProjectType "General"
    PS C:\> Get-PWWorkAreas -ProjectTypeName "General" -ProjectProperties 
    @{PROJECT_PropertyName = "Engineering Project"} 
    -PopulateProjectProperties
    
    First discovers the available property names for the "General" project 
    type, then uses one of those properties to filter work areas with 
    `Get-PWWorkAreas`.
    Example 7: Discover properties and filter work areas by multiple 
    properties
    
    PS C:\> Get-PWWorkAreaProperties -ProjectType "AllColumnTypes"
    PS C:\> Get-PWWorkAreas -ProjectTypeName "AllColumnTypes" 
    -ProjectProperties @{PROJECT_P_STRING = "Some Value"; 
    PROJECT_P_LONGINTEGER = 555} -PopulateProjectProperties
    
    Discovers available properties for the "AllColumnTypes" project type, 
    then filters work areas using multiple property values.
    
RELATED LINKS





--

## Get-PWWorkAreaResources


NAME
    Get-PWWorkAreaResources
    
SYNOPSIS
    Retrieves resource settings for a ProjectWise Work Area.
    
    
SYNTAX
    Get-PWWorkAreaResources [-InputFolder] <ProjectWiseFolder> 
    [[-ResourceType] {application | department | environment | storagearea | 
    view | workflow | workspaceprofile | renditionprofile}] 
    [<CommonParameters>]
    
    
DESCRIPTION
    The `Get-PWWorkAreaResources` cmdlet retrieves resource settings 
    configured for a ProjectWise Work Area. It returns a DataTable 
    containing information about the resources assigned to the Work Area, 
    including the resource type, resource name, and whether the resource is 
    explicitly set, inherited, or not configured.
    
    You can retrieve all resource settings at once or filter by a specific 
    resource type using the `-ResourceType` parameter.
    
    The cmdlet returns a DataTable with the following columns:
    
    - ResourceType : The type of resource (displayed in uppercase), such as 
    APPLICATION, DEPARTMENT, ENVIRONMENT, etc. - ResourceName : The name of 
    the assigned resource. - ResourceStatus : Indicates how the resource is 
    assigned:   - `Explicit` - The resource is explicitly set on this Work 
    Area.   - `Inheriting` - The resource is inherited from a parent Work 
    Area.   - `None` - No resource is configured for this type.
    

PARAMETERS
    -InputFolder <ProjectWiseFolder>
        Specifies the Work Area folder object to retrieve resource 
        information for. The folder must be a Work Area; otherwise, a 
        warning is displayed and the cmdlet returns `$false`.
        
        Required?                    true
        Position?                    0
        Default value                None
        Accept pipeline input?       True (ByValue)
        Accept wildcard characters?  false
        
    -ResourceType <String>
        Specifies the type of resource to retrieve. If not specified, all 
        resource types are returned.
        
        Valid values are:
        
        - `application` - Application resource settings
        
        - `department` - Department resource settings
        
        - `environment` - Environment resource settings
        
        - `storagearea` - Storage area resource settings
        
        - `view` - View resource settings
        
        - `workflow` - Workflow resource settings
        
        - `workspaceprofile` - Workspace profile resource settings
        
        - `renditionprofile` - Rendition profile resource settings
        
        Required?                    false
        Position?                    1
        Default value                None
        Accept pipeline input?       False
        Accept wildcard characters?  false
        
    <CommonParameters>
        This cmdlet supports the common parameters: Verbose, Debug,
        ErrorAction, ErrorVariable, WarningAction, WarningVariable,
        OutBuffer, PipelineVariable, and OutVariable. For more information, 
    see 
        about_CommonParameters 
    (https:/go.microsoft.com/fwlink/?LinkID=113216). 
    
INPUTS
    PWPS_DAB.CommonTypes+ProjectWiseFolder
        A ProjectWise folder object representing a Work Area. You can pipe a 
        folder object from cmdlets such as `Get-PWFolders` or 
        `Show-PWFolderBrowserDialog`.
    
    
OUTPUTS
    System.Data.DataTable
        Returns a DataTable containing resource information with the 
        following columns:
        
        | Column | Type | Description | |--------|------|-------------| | 
        ResourceType | String | The type of resource (uppercase), e.g., 
        APPLICATION, DEPARTMENT | | ResourceName | String | The name of the 
        assigned resource | | ResourceStatus | String | The assignment 
        status: Explicit, Inheriting, or None |
    
    System.Boolean
        Returns `$false` if the input folder is not a Work Area or if the 
        cmdlet fails to retrieve resource information.
    
    
NOTES
    
    
        - The input folder must be a Work Area. If a regular folder is 
        provided, the cmdlet displays a warning and returns `$false`.
        
        - Resource types in the output DataTable are displayed in uppercase 
        (e.g., APPLICATION, DEPARTMENT).
        
        - When a resource status is "None", the ResourceName will be empty 
        as no resource is configured for that type.
    
    EXAMPLE 1: Get all resource settings for a Work Area using folder browser
    
    Get-PWWorkAreaResources -InputFolder (Show-PWFolderBrowserDialog)
    
    Opens an interactive folder browser dialog to select a Work Area, then 
    retrieves all resource settings for the selected Work Area.
    - EXAMPLE 2: Get all resource settings for a Work Area by path -
    
    $workArea = Get-PWFolders -FolderPath 'Projects\Building Construction'
    Get-PWWorkAreaResources -InputFolder $workArea
    
    Retrieves all resource settings for the Work Area located at the 
    specified path.
    ------ EXAMPLE 3: Get application resource settings only ------
    
    $workArea = Get-PWFolders -FolderPath 'Projects\Building Construction'
    Get-PWWorkAreaResources -InputFolder $workArea -ResourceType application
    
    Retrieves only the application resource settings for the specified Work 
    Area.
    ------- EXAMPLE 4: Get department resource settings only -------
    
    $workArea = Get-PWFolders -FolderPath 'Projects\Building Construction'
    Get-PWWorkAreaResources -InputFolder $workArea -ResourceType department
    
    Retrieves only the department resource settings for the specified Work 
    Area.
    ------ EXAMPLE 5: Get environment resource settings only ------
    
    $workArea = Get-PWFolders -FolderPath 'Projects\Building Construction'
    Get-PWWorkAreaResources -InputFolder $workArea -ResourceType environment
    
    Retrieves only the environment resource settings for the specified Work 
    Area.
    ------ EXAMPLE 6: Get storage area resource settings only ------
    
    $workArea = Get-PWFolders -FolderPath 'Projects\Building Construction'
    Get-PWWorkAreaResources -InputFolder $workArea -ResourceType storagearea
    
    Retrieves only the storage area resource settings for the specified Work 
    Area.
    ---------- EXAMPLE 7: Get view resource settings only ----------
    
    $workArea = Get-PWFolders -FolderPath 'Projects\Building Construction'
    Get-PWWorkAreaResources -InputFolder $workArea -ResourceType view
    
    Retrieves only the view resource settings for the specified Work Area.
    -------- EXAMPLE 8: Get workflow resource settings only --------
    
    $workArea = Get-PWFolders -FolderPath 'Projects\Building Construction'
    Get-PWWorkAreaResources -InputFolder $workArea -ResourceType workflow
    
    Retrieves only the workflow resource settings for the specified Work 
    Area.
    --- EXAMPLE 9: Get workspace profile resource settings only ---
    
    $workArea = Get-PWFolders -FolderPath 'Projects\Building Construction'
    Get-PWWorkAreaResources -InputFolder $workArea -ResourceType 
    workspaceprofile
    
    Retrieves only the workspace profile resource settings for the specified 
    Work Area.
    --- EXAMPLE 10: Get rendition profile resource settings only ---
    
    $workArea = Get-PWFolders -FolderPath 'Projects\Building Construction'
    Get-PWWorkAreaResources -InputFolder $workArea -ResourceType 
    renditionprofile
    
    Retrieves only the rendition profile resource settings for the specified 
    Work Area.
    ---- EXAMPLE 11: Get resource settings using pipeline input ----
    
    Get-PWFolders -FolderPath 'Projects\Building Construction' | 
    Get-PWWorkAreaResources
    
    Pipes a Work Area folder object to the cmdlet and retrieves all resource 
    settings.
    ---- EXAMPLE 12: Get resource settings with verbose output ----
    
    $workArea = Get-PWFolders -FolderPath 'Projects\Building Construction'
    Get-PWWorkAreaResources -InputFolder $workArea -Verbose
    
    Retrieves all resource settings for the specified Work Area with verbose 
    output enabled for additional processing information.
    
RELATED LINKS

## Get-PWWorkAreaRoles


NAME
    Get-PWWorkAreaRoles
    
SYNOPSIS
    Retrieves participant roles assigned to a ProjectWise Work Area.
    
    
SYNTAX
    Get-PWWorkAreaRoles [-InputFolder] <ProjectWiseFolder[]> [-AccessMask] 
    [-Description] [-DocumentSecurity] [-FolderSecurity] [-RichProjectsOnly] 
    [<CommonParameters>]
    
    
DESCRIPTION
    The Get-PWWorkAreaRoles cmdlet retrieves all participant roles assigned 
    to a Work Area or folder. It returns information about users, groups, 
    and userlists that have access control applied, along with their 
    assigned roles such as Project Administrator, Project Leader, Project 
    Team Member, Project Observer, No Access, or Custom.
    
    By default, both folder and document security roles are returned. Use 
    the -FolderSecurity or -DocumentSecurity switches to return only one 
    type.
    

PARAMETERS
    -InputFolder <ProjectWiseFolder[]>
        Specifies the Work Area folder(s) to retrieve access control 
        information from. This parameter accepts pipeline input from cmdlets 
        such as Get-PWFolders.
        
        Required?                    true
        Position?                    0
        Default value                None
        Accept pipeline input?       True (ByValue)
        Accept wildcard characters?  false
        
    -AccessMask [<SwitchParameter>]
        When included, Access Mask information will be returned.
        
        Required?                    false
        Position?                    named
        Default value                False
        Accept pipeline input?       False
        Accept wildcard characters?  false
        
    -Description [<SwitchParameter>]
        When included, User, Group, or Access List description is returned.
        
        Required?                    false
        Position?                    named
        Default value                False
        Accept pipeline input?       False
        Accept wildcard characters?  false
        
    -DocumentSecurity [<SwitchParameter>]
        When included, only document access control information will be 
        returned. If neither -DocumentSecurity nor -FolderSecurity is 
        specified, both are returned.
        
        Required?                    false
        Position?                    named
        Default value                False
        Accept pipeline input?       False
        Accept wildcard characters?  false
        
    -FolderSecurity [<SwitchParameter>]
        When included, only folder/project access control information is 
        returned. If neither -FolderSecurity nor -DocumentSecurity is 
        specified, both are returned.
        
        Required?                    false
        Position?                    named
        Default value                False
        Accept pipeline input?       False
        Accept wildcard characters?  false
        
    -RichProjectsOnly [<SwitchParameter>]
        When included, only Work Area (Rich Project) access control will be 
        returned. Regular folders will be skipped.
        
        Required?                    false
        Position?                    named
        Default value                False
        Accept pipeline input?       False
        Accept wildcard characters?  false
        
    <CommonParameters>
        This cmdlet supports the common parameters: Verbose, Debug,
        ErrorAction, ErrorVariable, WarningAction, WarningVariable,
        OutBuffer, PipelineVariable, and OutVariable. For more information, 
    see 
        about_CommonParameters 
    (https:/go.microsoft.com/fwlink/?LinkID=113216). 
    
INPUTS
    PWPS_DAB.CommonTypes+ProjectWiseFolder[]
        You can pipe ProjectWiseFolder objects to this cmdlet.
    
    
OUTPUTS
    PWPS_DAB.Common.UserTypes.PWRole
        Returns PWRole objects with the following properties: - ItemType : 
        Indicates the security type - "Proj" for folder/project security or 
        "Doc" for document security. - Name : The name of the user, group, 
        or userlist that has access control applied. - Type : The member 
        type - "user", "group", or "userlist". - Role : The assigned role - 
        "Project Administrator", "Project Leader", "Project Team Member", 
        "Project Observer", "No Access", or "Custom".
    
    
NOTES
    
    
        - Both folder and document security roles are returned by default. 
        Use -FolderSecurity or -DocumentSecurity to return only one type.
        
        - The -RichProjectsOnly switch filters output to include only Work 
        Areas, excluding regular folders.
        
        - Use the -Verbose switch to see detailed processing information.
    
    ---- EXAMPLE 1: Get all roles from a Work Area via pipeline ----
    
    Get-PWFolders -FolderPath 'BSI900 - Adelaide Tower' -JustOne -Slow | 
    Get-PWWorkAreaRoles
    
    Retrieves all participant roles (both folder and document security) for 
    the specified Work Area.
    -------- EXAMPLE 2: Get roles from multiple Work Areas --------
    
    Get-PWFolders -FolderPath 'Projects\2024\*' -Slow | Get-PWWorkAreaRoles
    
    Retrieves participant roles for all folders matching the specified path 
    pattern.
    ---------- EXAMPLE 3: Get only folder security roles ----------
    
    Get-PWFolders -FolderPath 'BSI900 - Adelaide Tower' -JustOne -Slow | 
    Get-PWWorkAreaRoles -FolderSecurity
    
    Retrieves only the folder/project level security roles, excluding 
    document security roles.
    --------- EXAMPLE 4: Get only document security roles ---------
    
    Get-PWFolders -FolderPath 'BSI900 - Adelaide Tower' -JustOne -Slow | 
    Get-PWWorkAreaRoles -DocumentSecurity
    
    Retrieves only the document level security roles, excluding folder 
    security roles.
    ----------- EXAMPLE 5: Get roles for Work Areas only -----------
    
    Get-PWFolders -FolderPath 'Projects\2024\*' -Slow | Get-PWWorkAreaRoles 
    -RichProjectsOnly
    
    Retrieves participant roles only for folders that are Work Areas (Rich 
    Projects), skipping regular folders.
    --- EXAMPLE 6: Get folder security roles for Work Areas only ---
    
    Get-PWFolders -FolderPath 'Projects\2024\*' -Slow | Get-PWWorkAreaRoles 
    -FolderSecurity -RichProjectsOnly
    
    Retrieves only folder security roles and only for Work Areas, combining 
    both filters.
    
RELATED LINKS





--

## Get-PWWorkAreas


NAME
    Get-PWWorkAreas
    
SYNOPSIS
    Get-PWWorkAreas returns ProjectWise Folder Objects for all Work Areas 
    within the current datasource, a specified folder path, or matching a 
    Project Type and property values.
    
    
SYNTAX
    Get-PWWorkAreas [[-FolderPath] <String>] [[-ProjectTypeName] <String>] 
    [[-PopulateProjectProperties]] [[-JustOne]] [[-ProjectProperties] 
    <Hashtable>] [[-PopulatePaths]] [[-OnlyConnectedProjects]] 
    [[-OnlyNonConnectedProjects]] [<CommonParameters>]
    
    
DESCRIPTION
    Get-PWWorkAreas retrieves Work Areas from ProjectWise. You can retrieve 
    all Work Areas in the datasource, limit the search to a specific folder 
    tree, filter by Project Type, search by project property values, or 
    filter by connected/non-connected status.
    
    Use `-PopulatePaths` to include the full folder path in the returned 
    objects. Use `-PopulateProjectProperties` to include project property 
    values in the returned objects (note: this may slow down execution).
    

PARAMETERS
    -FolderPath <String>
        Specifies the folder path to search for Work Areas. When provided, 
        only Work Areas beneath this path are returned. If not specified, 
        all Work Areas in the datasource are searched.
        
        Required?                    false
        Position?                    0
        Default value                None
        Accept pipeline input?       False
        Accept wildcard characters?  false
        
    -ProjectTypeName <String>
        Specifies the Project Type name to filter Work Areas. This parameter 
        is required when using the `-ProjectProperties` parameter to search 
        by property values.
        
        Required?                    false
        Position?                    1
        Default value                None
        Accept pipeline input?       False
        Accept wildcard characters?  false
        
    -PopulateProjectProperties [<SwitchParameter>]
        When specified, populates the project property values on the 
        returned Work Area objects. Note: This may slow down execution as it 
        requires additional data retrieval for each Work Area.
        
        Required?                    false
        Position?                    2
        Default value                False
        Accept pipeline input?       False
        Accept wildcard characters?  false
        
    -JustOne [<SwitchParameter>]
        When specified with `-FolderPath`, returns only the Work Area at the 
        exact path specified rather than searching beneath it. If the 
        specified path is not a Work Area, a warning is displayed.
        
        Required?                    false
        Position?                    3
        Default value                False
        Accept pipeline input?       False
        Accept wildcard characters?  false
        
    -ProjectProperties <Hashtable>
        Specifies a hashtable of property name/value pairs to search for. 
        Property names are case-sensitive. The `%` character is supported as 
        a wildcard for string values. Requires the `-ProjectTypeName` 
        parameter to be specified.
        
        Example: `@{PROJECT_Project_Name='Highway%'; 
        PROJECT_Project_Number='12345'}`
        
        Required?                    false
        Position?                    4
        Default value                None
        Accept pipeline input?       False
        Accept wildcard characters?  false
        
    -PopulatePaths [<SwitchParameter>]
        When specified, populates the FullPath property on the returned Work 
        Area objects.
        
        Required?                    false
        Position?                    5
        Default value                False
        Accept pipeline input?       False
        Accept wildcard characters?  false
        
    -OnlyConnectedProjects [<SwitchParameter>]
        When specified, returns only Work Areas that are Connected Projects. 
        This parameter is mutually exclusive with 
        `-OnlyNonConnectedProjects`.
        
        Required?                    false
        Position?                    8
        Default value                False
        Accept pipeline input?       False
        Accept wildcard characters?  false
        
    -OnlyNonConnectedProjects [<SwitchParameter>]
        When specified, returns only Work Areas that are not Connected 
        Projects. This parameter is mutually exclusive with 
        `-OnlyConnectedProjects`.
        
        Required?                    false
        Position?                    9
        Default value                False
        Accept pipeline input?       False
        Accept wildcard characters?  false
        
    <CommonParameters>
        This cmdlet supports the common parameters: Verbose, Debug,
        ErrorAction, ErrorVariable, WarningAction, WarningVariable,
        OutBuffer, PipelineVariable, and OutVariable. For more information, 
    see 
        about_CommonParameters 
    (https:/go.microsoft.com/fwlink/?LinkID=113216). 
    
INPUTS
    None. This cmdlet does not accept pipeline input.
        
    
    
OUTPUTS
    CommonTypes.ProjectWiseFolder
        Returns one or more ProjectWiseFolder objects representing Work 
        Areas.
    
    
NOTES
    
    
        - The `-OnlyConnectedProjects` and `-OnlyNonConnectedProjects` 
        parameters are mutually exclusive. If both are specified, a warning 
        is displayed and no results are returned.
        
        - The `-ProjectProperties` parameter requires `-ProjectTypeName` to 
        be specified. If `-ProjectProperties` is provided without 
        `-ProjectTypeName`, a warning is displayed.
        
        - Property names in the `-ProjectProperties` hashtable are 
        case-sensitive.
        
        - Using `-PopulateProjectProperties` may significantly slow down 
        execution for large result sets.
    
    ------- EXAMPLE 1: Get all Work Areas in the datasource -------
    
    Get-PWWorkAreas
    
    Returns all Work Areas within the current ProjectWise datasource.
    - EXAMPLE 2: Get all Work Areas beneath a specific folder path -
    
    Get-PWWorkAreas -FolderPath 'Projects\Civil'
    
    Returns all Work Areas located beneath the specified folder path.
    --- EXAMPLE 3: Get all Work Areas with full paths populated ---
    
    Get-PWWorkAreas -PopulatePaths
    
    Returns all Work Areas with the FullPath property populated.
    ------- EXAMPLE 4: Get a single Work Area by exact path -------
    
    Get-PWWorkAreas -FolderPath 'Projects\Civil\Highway 101' -JustOne
    
    Returns only the Work Area at the specified path. If the path is not a 
    Work Area, a warning is displayed.
    --- EXAMPLE 5: Get all Work Areas of a specific Project Type ---
    
    Get-PWWorkAreas -ProjectTypeName 'General'
    
    Returns all Work Areas that are assigned the 'General' Project Type.
    - EXAMPLE 6: Get Work Areas with project properties populated -
    
    Get-PWWorkAreas -ProjectTypeName 'General' -PopulateProjectProperties
    
    Returns all Work Areas of the 'General' Project Type with their project 
    property values populated in the returned objects.
    EXAMPLE 7: Search Work Areas by property values using wildcard
    
    Get-PWWorkAreas -ProjectTypeName 'General' -ProjectProperties 
    @{PROJECT_Project_Name='Highway%'} -PopulateProjectProperties
    
    Returns Work Areas of the 'General' Project Type where the 
    PROJECT_Project_Name property starts with 'Highway'. The `%` character 
    is supported as a wildcard for string values. Property names are 
    case-sensitive.
    - EXAMPLE 8: Search Work Areas with multiple property criteria -
    
    Get-PWWorkAreas -ProjectTypeName 'AllColumnTypes' -ProjectProperties 
    @{PROJECT_P_STRING='Test Value'; PROJECT_P_LONGINTEGER=100}
    
    Returns Work Areas matching multiple property values. The 
    `-ProjectTypeName` parameter is required when using `-ProjectProperties`.
    EXAMPLE 9: Get Work Areas filtered by folder path and Project Type
    
    Get-PWWorkAreas -FolderPath 'Projects\Civil' -ProjectTypeName 'General' 
    -PopulatePaths
    
    Returns all Work Areas beneath the specified folder path that are 
    assigned the 'General' Project Type, with full paths populated.
    ----------- EXAMPLE 10: Get only Connected Projects -----------
    
    Get-PWWorkAreas -OnlyConnectedProjects
    
    Returns only Work Areas that are Connected Projects.
    --------- EXAMPLE 11: Get only Non-Connected Projects ---------
    
    Get-PWWorkAreas -OnlyNonConnectedProjects
    
    Returns only Work Areas that are not Connected Projects.
    EXAMPLE 12: Get Connected Projects beneath a folder path with paths 
    populated
    
    Get-PWWorkAreas -FolderPath 'Projects\Civil' -OnlyConnectedProjects 
    -PopulatePaths
    
    Returns Connected Projects beneath the specified folder path with full 
    paths populated.
    EXAMPLE 13: Combine Project Type filter with Connected Projects filter
    
    Get-PWWorkAreas -ProjectTypeName 'General' -OnlyConnectedProjects 
    -PopulateProjectProperties
    
    Returns Connected Projects of the 'General' Project Type with project 
    properties populated.
    
RELATED LINKS

## Get-PWWorkAreaTemplates


NAME
    Get-PWWorkAreaTemplates
    
SYNOPSIS
    Gets the work area templates in the current datasource.
    
    
SYNTAX
    Get-PWWorkAreaTemplates [<CommonParameters>]
    
    
DESCRIPTION
    Retrieves all work area templates configured in the current ProjectWise 
    datasource. Work area templates are decorated projects (work areas) 
    located in the system-defined templates folder. Each template is 
    returned as a ProjectWiseFolder object containing properties such as 
    Name, Description, ProjectID, Workflow, Environment, and Storage 
    information.
    

PARAMETERS
    <CommonParameters>
        This cmdlet supports the common parameters: Verbose, Debug,
        ErrorAction, ErrorVariable, WarningAction, WarningVariable,
        OutBuffer, PipelineVariable, and OutVariable. For more information, 
    see 
        about_CommonParameters 
    (https:/go.microsoft.com/fwlink/?LinkID=113216). 
    
INPUTS
    None
        This cmdlet does not accept any input.
    
    
OUTPUTS
    PWPS_DAB.CommonTypes+ProjectWiseFolder[]
        Returns an array of ProjectWise folder objects representing the work 
        area templates. Each object contains properties such as: - 
        ProjectID: The unique identifier of the template folder.
        
        - Name: The name of the template.
        
        - Description: The description of the template.
        
        - Workflow: The workflow associated with the template.
        
        - Environment: The environment associated with the template.
        
        - Storage: The storage area associated with the template.
        
        - IsRichProject: Indicates whether the folder is a work area (always 
        true for templates).
    
    
NOTES
    
    
        - This cmdlet retrieves only work areas from the system-defined 
        templates folder.
        
        - The cmdlet does not accept any parameters and returns all 
        available work area templates.
    
    -------------------------- EXAMPLE 1 --------------------------
    
    Get-PWWorkAreaTemplates
    
    Gets all work area templates in the current datasource.
    -------------------------- EXAMPLE 2 --------------------------
    
    $templates = Get-PWWorkAreaTemplates
    
    Stores all work area templates in a variable for further processing.
    
RELATED LINKS





--

## Get-PWWorkAreaTypes


NAME
    Get-PWWorkAreaTypes
    
SYNOPSIS
    Get-PWWorkAreaTypes is used to get information pertaining to all 
    ProjectWise Work Area Types within the active datasource.
    
    
SYNTAX
    Get-PWWorkAreaTypes [<CommonParameters>]
    
    
DESCRIPTION
    There are no parameters required.
    

PARAMETERS
    <CommonParameters>
        This cmdlet supports the common parameters: Verbose, Debug,
        ErrorAction, ErrorVariable, WarningAction, WarningVariable,
        OutBuffer, PipelineVariable, and OutVariable. For more information, 
    see 
        about_CommonParameters 
    (https:/go.microsoft.com/fwlink/?LinkID=113216). 
    
INPUTS
    
OUTPUTS
    
NOTES
    
    
        
    
    -------------------------- EXAMPLE 1 --------------------------
    
    #Example usage for getting all of the Work Area Types.
    Get-PWWorkAreaTypes
    #Example usage for getting specific Work Area Types
    Get-PWWorkAreaTypes | Where-Object {$_.SystemClass -eq "1" -and 
    $_.ClassDesc -ne "PickLists"}

## Import-PWAAMFile


NAME
    Import-PWAAMFile
    
SYNOPSIS
    Imports environments, workflows, applications, or saved searches from an 
    AAM file into the current ProjectWise datasource.
    
    
SYNTAX
    Import-PWAAMFile [-ImportFile] <string> [[-Environments]] 
    [[-WorkflowDefinitions]] [[-Applications]] [[-Searches]] [[-LogFile] 
    <string>] [<CommonParameters>]
    
    
DESCRIPTION
    Imports environments, workflows, applications, or saved searches from an 
    AAM file into the current ProjectWise datasource. It imports 
    environments if none are selected.
    
    Provide the AAM file and, if needed, a log file. The command returns no 
    output.
    
    Run it in a 32-bit PowerShell with the ProjectWise Import Wizard 
    available.
    

PARAMETERS
    -ImportFile <string>
        Full path to output AAM file. (Required).
        
        Required?                    true
        Position?                    0
        Default value                None
        Accept pipeline input?       False
        Accept wildcard characters?  false
        
    -Environments [<SwitchParameter>]
        Set to import environments.
        
        Required?                    false
        Position?                    1
        Default value                False
        Accept pipeline input?       False
        Accept wildcard characters?  false
        
    -WorkflowDefinitions [<SwitchParameter>]
        Set to impot workflows.
        
        Required?                    false
        Position?                    2
        Default value                False
        Accept pipeline input?       False
        Accept wildcard characters?  false
        
    -Applications [<SwitchParameter>]
        Set to import applications.
        
        Required?                    false
        Position?                    3
        Default value                False
        Accept pipeline input?       False
        Accept wildcard characters?  false
        
    -Searches [<SwitchParameter>]
        Set to import searches.
        
        Required?                    false
        Position?                    4
        Default value                False
        Accept pipeline input?       False
        Accept wildcard characters?  false
        
    -LogFile <string>
        Full path to the import operation log file.
        
        Required?                    false
        Position?                    5
        Default value                None
        Accept pipeline input?       False
        Accept wildcard characters?  false
        
    <CommonParameters>
        This cmdlet supports the common parameters: Verbose, Debug,
        ErrorAction, ErrorVariable, WarningAction, WarningVariable,
        OutBuffer, PipelineVariable, and OutVariable. For more information, 
    see 
        about_CommonParameters 
    (https:/go.microsoft.com/fwlink/?LinkID=113216). 
    
INPUTS
    
OUTPUTS
    
NOTES
    
    
        
    
    -------------------------- EXAMPLE 1 --------------------------
    
    Import-PWAAMFile "c:\temp\environments.aam"
    
    
    
RELATED LINKS

## Import-PWDocuments


NAME
    Import-PWDocuments
    
SYNOPSIS
    Imports local files into ProjectWise.
    
    
SYNTAX
    Import-PWDocuments [-InputFolder] <string> [[-ProjectWiseFolder] 
    <string>] [[-ExcludeSourceDirectoryFromTargetPath]] 
    [[-IncludeEmptyFolders]] [[-FoldersOnly]] [[-ServerIsUtc]] 
    [[-SkipDateCheck]] [[-SuppressOutput]] [[-ValidationReport] <string>] 
    [[-Exclusions] <string[]>] [[-MultiThreaded]] [[-ExtensionsOfInterest] 
    <string>] [[-DefaultStorage] <string>] [[-DefaultWorkflow] <string>] 
    [[-DefaultEnvironment] <string>] [[-DefaultState] <string>] 
    [[-MakeProjectWiseMatchDisk]] [[-CreateVersions]] [[-Overwrite]] 
    [<CommonParameters>]
    
    
DESCRIPTION
    Imports files from a local or network folder into a ProjectWise folder 
    tree. Use inclusion and exclusion options to control which files are 
    imported.
    
    Choose storage, workflow, environment, and state defaults when the 
    target folder does not supply them. Use `ValidationReport` to produce a 
    report.
    
    `MakeProjectWiseMatchDisk` can delete ProjectWise documents that are 
    absent from disk. Check the target and options carefully before using it.
    

PARAMETERS
    -InputFolder <string>
        Path to the folder on disk to import from. Can be a UNC Path or can 
        be a long path specification (\\?\c:\, \\?\UNC\Server\Share\)
        
        Required?                    true
        Position?                    0
        Default value                None
        Accept pipeline input?       True (ByValue)
        Accept wildcard characters?  false
        
    -ProjectWiseFolder <string>
        ProjectWise folder path (e.g. "Target A\Subfolder 1\Sub Sub Folder 
        2").
        
        Required?                    false
        Position?                    1
        Default value                None
        Accept pipeline input?       True (ByValue)
        Accept wildcard characters?  false
        
    -ExtensionsOfInterest <string>
        Semi-colon delimited filter for the files from the disk to INCLUDE 
        (e.g. ".dgn;.dwg").
        
        Required?                    false
        Position?                    2
        Default value                None
        Accept pipeline input?       False
        Accept wildcard characters?  false
        
    -Exclusions <string[]>
        Filenames and patterns to exclude (e.g. "thumbs.db"," .lnk"," .tmp").
        
        Required?                    false
        Position?                    17
        Default value                None
        Accept pipeline input?       False
        Accept wildcard characters?  false
        
    -DefaultStorage <string>
        Set default storage for new folders to be created.
        
        Required?                    false
        Position?                    3
        Default value                None
        Accept pipeline input?       False
        Accept wildcard characters?  false
        
    -DefaultWorkflow <string>
        Set default workflow for new folders to be created.
        
        Required?                    false
        Position?                    4
        Default value                None
        Accept pipeline input?       False
        Accept wildcard characters?  false
        
    -DefaultEnvironment <string>
        Set default environment for new folders to be created.
        
        Required?                    false
        Position?                    5
        Default value                None
        Accept pipeline input?       False
        Accept wildcard characters?  false
        
    -DefaultState <string>
        Set default workflow state for new documents being created.
        
        Required?                    false
        Position?                    6
        Default value                None
        Accept pipeline input?       False
        Accept wildcard characters?  false
        
    -MakeProjectWiseMatchDisk [<SwitchParameter>]
        Delete documents in ProjectWise that are not present on disk. Useful 
        for mirroring data from disk.
        
        Required?                    false
        Position?                    7
        Default value                False
        Accept pipeline input?       False
        Accept wildcard characters?  false
        
    -CreateVersions [<SwitchParameter>]
        Set to create a version when the file already exists in ProjectWise. 
        If not set, will just overwrite.
        
        Required?                    false
        Position?                    8
        Default value                False
        Accept pipeline input?       False
        Accept wildcard characters?  false
        
    -Overwrite [<SwitchParameter>]
        Set to create a version when the file already exists in ProjectWise. 
        If not set, will just overwrite.
        
        Required?                    false
        Position?                    9
        Default value                False
        Accept pipeline input?       False
        Accept wildcard characters?  false
        
    -ExcludeSourceDirectoryFromTargetPath [<SwitchParameter>]
        Set this to exclude the source directory from the target path. So, 
        if set, then D:\Export\Folder1\file1.dgn becomes 'ProjectWise 
        Parent\Folder1\file1.dgn'
        
        Required?                    false
        Position?                    10
        Default value                False
        Accept pipeline input?       False
        Accept wildcard characters?  false
        
    -IncludeEmptyFolders [<SwitchParameter>]
        Foolishly include empty folders
        
        Required?                    false
        Position?                    11
        Default value                False
        Accept pipeline input?       False
        Accept wildcard characters?  false
        
    -FoldersOnly [<SwitchParameter>]
        Replicate folders from disk
        
        Required?                    false
        Position?                    12
        Default value                False
        Accept pipeline input?       False
        Accept wildcard characters?  false
        
    -ServerIsUtc [<SwitchParameter>]
        For date matching
        
        Required?                    false
        Position?                    13
        Default value                False
        Accept pipeline input?       False
        Accept wildcard characters?  false
        
    -SkipDateCheck [<SwitchParameter>]
        Skip the date check for existing files.
        
        Required?                    false
        Position?                    14
        Default value                False
        Accept pipeline input?       False
        Accept wildcard characters?  false
        
    -SuppressOutput [<SwitchParameter>]
        There's some thought that this might improve performance.
        
        Required?                    false
        Position?                    15
        Default value                False
        Accept pipeline input?       False
        Accept wildcard characters?  false
        
    -ValidationReport <string>
        Full path to XLSX file for output (c:\temp\ImportValidation.xlsx).
        
        Required?                    false
        Position?                    16
        Default value                None
        Accept pipeline input?       False
        Accept wildcard characters?  false
        
    -MultiThreaded [<SwitchParameter>]
        Defaults to single-threaded (not used). If this switch is used, the 
        import runs multi-threaded which may improve performance.
        
        Required?                    false
        Position?                    17
        Default value                False
        Accept pipeline input?       False
        Accept wildcard characters?  false
        
    <CommonParameters>
        This cmdlet supports the common parameters: Verbose, Debug,
        ErrorAction, ErrorVariable, WarningAction, WarningVariable,
        OutBuffer, PipelineVariable, and OutVariable. For more information, 
    see 
        about_CommonParameters 
    (https:/go.microsoft.com/fwlink/?LinkID=113216). 
    
INPUTS
    System.String
        Path to the folder on disk to import from. Can be a UNC Path or can 
        be a long path specification (\\?\c:\, \\?\UNC\Server\Share\)
    
    System.String
        ProjectWise folder path (e.g. "Target A\Subfolder 1\Sub Sub Folder 
        2").
    
    
OUTPUTS
    
NOTES
    
    
        
    
    -------------------------- EXAMPLE 1 --------------------------
    
    #Example usage for reporting on documents.
    #Will import the documents from the input folder with one of the 
    matching extensions into PW under the give ProjectWise folder. Creates 
    folders as necessary.
    #Creates versions of duplicate files with the given switch.
    Import-PWDocuments -InputFolder "c:\files\subfolder" -ProjectWiseFolder 
    "Target A\Folder 1" -ExtensionsOfInterest ".dgn;.dwg" -DefaultStorage 
    Store01 -CreateVersions
    
    
    -------------------------- EXAMPLE 2 --------------------------
    
    #Will import files from Input folder. Shows use of pipeline.
    Import-PWDocuments -InputFolder "c:\files" -ProjectWiseFolder "Target A" 
    -DefaultStorage Store01 -DefaultWorkflow Normal | Set-PWDocumentState 
    -State "Archived"
    #Import documents with exclusions.
    $docs = Import-PWDocuments -InputFolder 'W:\Import Test' 
    -ProjectWiseFolder 'Import test for rob\Test 03' -DefaultStorage Storage 
    -Overwrite -Exclusions 'Amason S3 Keys.txt','msconfigreport.txt'
    
    
    
RELATED LINKS
