## Set-PWFolderView


NAME
    Set-PWFolderView
    
SYNOPSIS
    Sets the default View and/or PreviewPaneView for the specified 
    folder(s). Can also remove the default view settings when the Remove 
    switch is specified.
    
    
SYNTAX
    Set-PWFolderView [-InputFolder] <ProjectWiseFolder[]> [[-View] <String>] 
    [[-PreviewPaneView] <String>] [-Remove] [<CommonParameters>]
    
    
DESCRIPTION
    Sets the default View and/or PreviewPaneView for the specified 
    folder(s). Can also remove the default view settings when the Remove 
    switch is specified. At least one of the View or PreviewPaneView 
    parameters must be provided. The view name must be a valid global view 
    that exists in ProjectWise.
    

PARAMETERS
    -InputFolder <ProjectWiseFolder[]>
        ProjectWise folder object(s) to set the view for. Can be obtained 
        using Get-PWFolders or Show-PWFolderBrowserDialog.
        
        Required?                    true
        Position?                    0
        Default value                None
        Accept pipeline input?       True (ByValue)
        Accept wildcard characters?  false
        
    -View <String>
        Name of view to set as default view for the specified folder(s). 
        Must be a valid global view name.
        
        Required?                    false
        Position?                    1
        Default value                None
        Accept pipeline input?       False
        Accept wildcard characters?  false
        
    -PreviewPaneView <String>
        Name of view to set as default PreviewPaneView for the specified 
        folder(s). Must be a valid global view name.
        
        Required?                    false
        Position?                    2
        Default value                None
        Accept pipeline input?       False
        Accept wildcard characters?  false
        
    -Remove [<SwitchParameter>]
        When specified, removes the default View and/or PreviewPaneView from 
        the specified folder(s) instead of setting them.
        
        Required?                    false
        Position?                    named
        Default value                False
        Accept pipeline input?       False
        Accept wildcard characters?  false
        
    <CommonParameters>
        This cmdlet supports the common parameters: Verbose, Debug,
        ErrorAction, ErrorVariable, WarningAction, WarningVariable,
        OutBuffer, PipelineVariable, and OutVariable. For more information, 
    see 
        about_CommonParameters 
    (https:/go.microsoft.com/fwlink/?LinkID=113216). 
    
INPUTS
    PWPS_DAB.CommonTypes+ProjectWiseFolder[]
        ProjectWise folder object(s) can be piped to this cmdlet.
    
    
OUTPUTS
    None
        This cmdlet does not generate any output. Use the -Verbose switch to 
        see detailed information about the operations performed.
    
    
NOTES
    
    
        - At least one of the View or PreviewPaneView parameters must be 
        provided.
        
        - The view name must be a valid global view that exists in 
        ProjectWise.
        
        - When using the Remove switch, the view name is still required to 
        identify which type of view setting to remove.
        
        - Folders with an invalid ProjectID (less than 1) are skipped during 
        processing.
    
    -------------------------- EXAMPLE 1 --------------------------
    
    $folder = Show-PWFolderBrowserDialog
    Set-PWFolderView -InputFolder $folder -View 'Default' -Verbose
    
    Sets the default View for a folder selected via folder browser dialog.
    -------------------------- EXAMPLE 2 --------------------------
    
    $folder = Get-PWFolders -FolderPath 'Projects\Civil' -JustOne
    Set-PWFolderView -InputFolder $folder -View 'Default' -Verbose
    
    Sets the default View for a folder retrieved by path.
    -------------------------- EXAMPLE 3 --------------------------
    
    $folder = Show-PWFolderBrowserDialog
    Set-PWFolderView -InputFolder $folder -PreviewPaneView 'Default' -Verbose
    
    Sets the default PreviewPaneView for a folder selected via folder 
    browser dialog.
    -------------------------- EXAMPLE 4 --------------------------
    
    $folder = Get-PWFolders -FolderPath 'Projects\Civil' -JustOne
    Set-PWFolderView -InputFolder $folder -PreviewPaneView 'Default' -Verbose
    
    Sets the default PreviewPaneView for a folder retrieved by path.
    -------------------------- EXAMPLE 5 --------------------------
    
    $folder = Get-PWFolders -FolderPath 'Projects\Civil' -JustOne
    Set-PWFolderView -InputFolder $folder -View 'Default' -PreviewPaneView 
    'Default' -Verbose
    
    Sets both the default View and PreviewPaneView for a folder.
    -------------------------- EXAMPLE 6 --------------------------
    
    $folder = Get-PWFolders -FolderPath 'Projects\Civil' -JustOne
    Set-PWFolderView -InputFolder $folder -View 'Default' -Remove -Verbose
    
    Removes the default View from a folder.
    -------------------------- EXAMPLE 7 --------------------------
    
    $folder = Get-PWFolders -FolderPath 'Projects\Civil' -JustOne
    Set-PWFolderView -InputFolder $folder -PreviewPaneView 'Default' -Remove 
    -Verbose
    
    Removes the default PreviewPaneView from a folder.
    -------------------------- EXAMPLE 8 --------------------------
    
    $folder = Get-PWFolders -FolderPath 'Projects\Civil' -JustOne
    Set-PWFolderView -InputFolder $folder -View 'Default' -PreviewPaneView 
    'Default' -Remove -Verbose
    
    Removes both the default View and PreviewPaneView from a folder.
    -------------------------- EXAMPLE 9 --------------------------
    
    $folders = Get-PWFolders -FolderPath 'Projects\Civil'
    $folders | Set-PWFolderView -View 'Default' -Verbose
    
    Sets the default View for multiple folders using pipeline input.
    -------------------------- EXAMPLE 10 --------------------------
    
    $folders = Get-PWFolders -FolderPath 'Projects\Civil'
    $folders | Set-PWFolderView -View 'Default' -Remove -Verbose
    
    Removes the default View from multiple folders using pipeline input.
    
RELATED LINKS

## Set-PWLocalFileEAttributes


NAME
    Set-PWLocalFileEAttributes
    
SYNOPSIS
    Sets attributes embedded in a local file.
    
    
SYNTAX
    Set-PWLocalFileEAttributes -MIME <String> -Path <String> -ValueObjects 
    <PSObject[]> [<CommonParameters>]
    
    
DESCRIPTION
    Sets ProjectWise-compatible environment attribute values in a local 
    file. Provide the file path, MIME type, and attribute values.
    
    Each value must include Class, Name, and Value. The cmdlet returns the 
    updated attribute values.
    
    The command does not require a ProjectWise connection. It changes the 
    specified local file.
    

PARAMETERS

## Set-PWLookupData


NAME
    Set-PWLookupData
    
SYNOPSIS
    Replaces whole data set for one lookup registered with default lookup 
    data storage
    
    
SYNTAX
    Set-PWLookupData [-LookupName] <String> [[-LookupRow] <PSObject>] 
    [<CommonParameters>]
    
    
DESCRIPTION
    Replaces whole data set for the specified lookup. Will fail if lookup 
    does not exist or is of a diffenet type.
    

PARAMETERS
    -LookupName <String>
        Name of the lookup to replace the data
        
        Required?                    true
        Position?                    0
        Default value                None
        Accept pipeline input?       True (ByPropertyName)
        Accept wildcard characters?  false
        
    -LookupRow <PSObject>
        A row data for a replacement {column = value list}. Multiple rows 
        can by passed via pipeline in a single update. Prior to replacement 
        all existing data is cleaned for the lookup. After operation lookup 
        will only contain the data submitted with this operation. Columns 
        that do not receive data in the row will be set to null, and will 
        show as empty values during lookup selects. Providing data for 
        non-existing columns will result in failed operation.
        
        Required?                    false
        Position?                    1
        Default value                None
        Accept pipeline input?       True (ByValue, ByPropertyName)
        Accept wildcard characters?  false
        
    <CommonParameters>
        This cmdlet supports the common parameters: Verbose, Debug,
        ErrorAction, ErrorVariable, WarningAction, WarningVariable,
        OutBuffer, PipelineVariable, and OutVariable. For more information, 
    see 
        about_CommonParameters 
    (https:/go.microsoft.com/fwlink/?LinkID=113216). 
    
INPUTS
    
OUTPUTS
    
NOTES
    
    
        
    
    ------------------------- FillFromCsv -------------------------
    
    $FileColumns = 'state_code', 'county', 'municipality', 'name', 
    'sort_order' 
    Import-Csv -Path "C:\varios\values.txt" -Header $FileColumns `
        | Set-PWLookupData -LookupName "my.towns"
    
    Fills lookup "my.towns" with data from a csv file that has no header row
    ----------------------- FillFromDatabase -----------------------
    
    $ServerInstance = "(local)"
    $Database = "db1_demo" 
    $Query = "SELECT PW_CODE as code, PW_DESCRIPTION as description, 
    PW_SORTORDER as sortorder, cde_state as cde_state FROM lkp_pw_attributes 
    where PW_FILTER = 'Status'"
    
    Invoke-Sqlcmd -ServerInstance $ServerInstance -Database $Database -Query 
    $Query `
         | Select * -ExcludeProperty RowError, RowState, Table, ItemArray, 
    HasErrors `
         | Set-PWLookupData -LookupName "my.lookup.from.db"
    
    Fills lookup using database select; Drops diagnostics columns returned 
    by Invoke-Sqlcmd before passing to lookup
    ------------------------ FillFromExcel ------------------------
    
    # !! need 3-rd party module ImportExcel; Need to install it once to be 
    able to try this!
    # command: Install-Module -Name ImportExcel
    
    Import-Excel -Path "C:\sheets\my-rules.xlsx" -WorksheetName 'Rules' 
    -StartRow 6 -EndColumn 4 `
        | Set-PWLookupData -LookupName 'AZ.Wre'
    
    Imports selected area from excel sheet, taking the StartRow as header
    ------------------------- FillFromJson -------------------------
    
    $CitiesJson = "https://where-you-can-find-it/US_States_and_Cities.json"
    
    (Invoke-WebRequest $CitiesJson | ConvertFrom-Json).psobject.properties `
        | select @{n='State'; e={$_.name}}, @{n='Cities'; e={$_.value}}  `
        | select -Property State -Expand Cities `
        | select State, @{n='City'; e={$_}} `
        | Set-PWLookupData -LookupName "States_and_Cities"
    
    A complicated Json download and manipulation example that formats lookup 
    rows from a nested json
    
RELATED LINKS

## Set-PWSession


NAME
    Set-PWSession
    
SYNOPSIS
    Set the active ProjectWise session to a specified datasource.
    
    
SYNTAX
    Set-PWSession [-Datasource] <string> [<CommonParameters>]
    
    
DESCRIPTION
    The Set-PWSession cmdlet switches the active ProjectWise session to a 
    specified datasource. The datasource must already be logged in and 
    available in the current sessions list. The Datasource value must be in 
    the format ServerName:DatasourceName (e.g., PWServer01:ProjectWise).
    
    If the specified datasource is not found in the active sessions, a 
    warning is displayed along with a list of available sessions. If no 
    sessions are currently active, a warning is displayed indicating that no 
    sessions exist.
    

PARAMETERS
    -Datasource <string>
        The datasource to switch to. Must be in the format 
        ServerName:DatasourceName and must match an active session 
        (Required).
        
        Required?                    true
        Position?                    0
        Default value                None
        Accept pipeline input?       True (ByValue)
        Accept wildcard characters?  false
        
    <CommonParameters>
        This cmdlet supports the common parameters: Verbose, Debug,
        ErrorAction, ErrorVariable, WarningAction, WarningVariable,
        OutBuffer, PipelineVariable, and OutVariable. For more information, 
    see 
        about_CommonParameters 
    (https:/go.microsoft.com/fwlink/?LinkID=113216). 
    
INPUTS
    System.String
        The datasource to switch to (Required). A datasource name "string" 
        can be piped to Set-PWSession.
    
    
OUTPUTS
    System.String
        Returns a string indicating the active datasource upon successful 
        switch.
    
    
NOTES
    
    
        
    
    -------------------------- EXAMPLE 1 --------------------------
    
    Set-PWSession -Datasource "PWServer01:ProjectWise"
    
    Switches the active session to the ProjectWise datasource on PWServer01. 
    The Datasource value must include the server name and datasource name 
    separated by a colon.
    -------------------------- EXAMPLE 2 --------------------------
    
    Set-PWSession "PWServer01:ProjectWise"
    
    Switches the active session using the positional parameter. The 
    Datasource value must match an active session exactly.
    -------------------------- EXAMPLE 3 --------------------------
    
    "PWServer01:ProjectWise" | Set-PWSession
    
    Pipes the datasource name to Set-PWSession to switch the active session.
    
RELATED LINKS





--

## Set-PWUserDynamicCreationFilter


NAME
    Set-PWUserDynamicCreationFilter
    
SYNOPSIS
    Sets the User Dynamic Creation Filter of the current ProjectWise 
    Datasource.
    
    
SYNTAX
    Set-PWUserDynamicCreationFilter -Clear [<CommonParameters>]
    
    Set-PWUserDynamicCreationFilter [-Filter] <String> [<CommonParameters>]
    
    
DESCRIPTION
    The Set-PWUserDynamicCreationFilter cmdlet validates and sets the 
    dynamic creation filter of the current ProjectWise Datasource 
    overwriting the previous setting. You can use the -Clear flag to clear 
    the filter.
    

PARAMETERS
    -Clear [<SwitchParameter>]
        Clears the user dynamic creation filter.
        
        Required?                    true
        Position?                    named
        Default value                False
        Accept pipeline input?       True (ByValue)
        Accept wildcard characters?  false
        
    -Filter <String>
        The user dynamic creation filter. The -Filter flag is optional.
        
        Note: the full filter value must be enclosed in single quotes (') 
        and separate filter parameter values (like name, organization, etc.) 
        must be enclosed in double quotes (").
        
        Required?                    true
        Position?                    0
        Default value                None
        Accept pipeline input?       True (ByValue)
        Accept wildcard characters?  false
        
    <CommonParameters>
        This cmdlet supports the common parameters: Verbose, Debug,
        ErrorAction, ErrorVariable, WarningAction, WarningVariable,
        OutBuffer, PipelineVariable, and OutVariable. For more information, 
    see 
        about_CommonParameters 
    (https:/go.microsoft.com/fwlink/?LinkID=113216). 
    
INPUTS
    String
        The user dynamic creation filter.
    
    
OUTPUTS
    None
        
    
    
NOTES
    
    
        Set-PWUserDynamicCreationFilter requires an open ProjectWise 
        Connection in the current thread. You can open a Connection with 
        Open-PWConnection before calling Set-PWUserDynamicCreationFilter.
    
    -------------------------- Example 1 --------------------------
    
    Set-PWUserDynamicCreationFilter 'organization = "Bentley Systems Inc" 
    AND group = "PW Users" OR organization = "Bentley Systems Inc" AND group 
    = "Administrators"'
    
    Sets the user creation filter to accept only users which belong to the 
    "Bentley Systems Inc" organization AND belong to either the "PW Users" 
    OR the "Administrators" group.
    
    Note: clauses "()" are not supported.
    -------------------------- Example 2 --------------------------
    
    Set-PWUserDynamicCreationFilter -Clear
    
    Clears the user dynamic creation filter.
    
RELATED LINKS

## Set-PWUserListOwners


NAME
    Set-PWUserListOwners
    
SYNOPSIS
    Adds a user or user list as an owner of a specified ProjectWise UserList.
    
    
SYNTAX
    Set-PWUserListOwners [-UserListName] <String> [-OwnerType] <String> 
    [-Owner] <String> [<CommonParameters>]
    
    Set-PWUserListOwners [-UserList] <UserList> [-OwnerType] <String> 
    [-Owner] <String> [<CommonParameters>]
    
    
DESCRIPTION
    The Set-PWUserListOwners cmdlet adds an owner to a ProjectWise user 
    list. You can specify the target user list either by its name (as a 
    string) or by passing a UserList object. The owner being added can be 
    either a user or another user list, as indicated by the OwnerType 
    parameter.
    
    If the specified owner is already an owner of the target user list, the 
    cmdlet will display a warning message instead of adding a duplicate 
    entry.
    
    On successful addition, the cmdlet returns a confirmation message 
    indicating that the owner was added.
    

PARAMETERS
    -UserListName <String>
        The name of the target user list to which the owner will be added. 
        Use this parameter when you want to specify the user list by its 
        name as a string. This parameter cannot be used together with the 
        UserList parameter.
        
        Required?                    true
        Position?                    0
        Default value                None
        Accept pipeline input?       True (ByValue)
        Accept wildcard characters?  false
        
    -UserList <UserList>
        A UserList object that specifies the target user list to which the 
        owner will be added. Use Get-PWUserList to retrieve a UserList 
        object. This parameter cannot be used together with the UserListName 
        parameter.
        
        Required?                    true
        Position?                    0
        Default value                None
        Accept pipeline input?       True (ByValue)
        Accept wildcard characters?  false
        
    -OwnerType <String>
        Specifies the type of the owner being added. This tells the cmdlet 
        whether the Owner parameter refers to a user or a user list. Valid 
        values are: - 'u' or 'user' � the owner is a user
        
        - 'ul' or 'userlist' � the owner is a user list
        
        
        The short forms ('u', 'ul') and long forms ('user', 'userlist') are 
        interchangeable.
        
        
        Required?                    true
        Position?                    1
        Default value                None
        Accept pipeline input?       False
        Accept wildcard characters?  false
        
    -Owner <String>
        The name of the user or user list to add as an owner of the target 
        user list. The value must match an existing user or user list in the 
        system, depending on what is specified in the OwnerType parameter.
        
        Required?                    true
        Position?                    1
        Default value                None
        Accept pipeline input?       False
        Accept wildcard characters?  false
        
    <CommonParameters>
        This cmdlet supports the common parameters: Verbose, Debug,
        ErrorAction, ErrorVariable, WarningAction, WarningVariable,
        OutBuffer, PipelineVariable, and OutVariable. For more information, 
    see 
        about_CommonParameters 
    (https:/go.microsoft.com/fwlink/?LinkID=113216). 
    
INPUTS
    System.String (UserListName)
        The name of the target user list. Required when using the 
        UserListName parameter set.
    
    PWPS_Cloud.UserList (UserList)
        A UserList object representing the target user list. Required when 
        using the UserList parameter set.
    
    System.String (OwnerType)
        The type of the owner being added. Required in all parameter sets. 
        Valid values are 'u', 'user', 'ul', or 'userlist'.
    
    System.String (Owner)
        The name of the user or user list to add as an owner. Required in 
        all parameter sets.
    
    
OUTPUTS
    System.String
        Returns a success message in the format: "Successfully added 
        '{Owner}' to UserList '{UserListName}' Owners."
        
        If the owner already exists, no output is returned and a warning 
        message is displayed instead.
    
    
NOTES
    
    
        - You cannot use both UserListName and UserList parameters in the 
        same command.
        
        - The Owner must be an existing user or user list in the system. If 
        the specified owner does not exist, an error is written.
        
        - If the specified target user list name does not exist, an error is 
        written.
        
        - If the owner is already assigned to the target user list, a 
        warning message is displayed and no changes are made.
        
        - The OwnerType only accepts 'u', 'user', 'ul', or 'userlist'. Group 
        ownership is not supported through this cmdlet.
        
        
        ## RELATED LINKS
        
    
    -------------------------- EXAMPLE 1 --------------------------
    
    Set-PWUserListOwners -UserListName 'MyUserList' -Owner 'TestUser' 
    -OwnerType 'u'
    
    Adds the user 'TestUser' as an owner of the user list 'MyUserList' using 
    the short form 'u' for OwnerType.
    -------------------------- EXAMPLE 2 --------------------------
    
    Set-PWUserListOwners -UserListName 'MyUserList' -Owner 'TestUser' 
    -OwnerType 'user'
    
    Adds the user 'TestUser' as an owner of the user list 'MyUserList' using 
    the long form 'user' for OwnerType. This is functionally identical to 
    Example 1.
    -------------------------- EXAMPLE 3 --------------------------
    
    Set-PWUserListOwners -UserListName 'MyUserList' -Owner 'AdminList' 
    -OwnerType 'ul'
    
    Adds the user list 'AdminList' as an owner of the user list 'MyUserList' 
    using the short form 'ul' for OwnerType.
    -------------------------- EXAMPLE 4 --------------------------
    
    Set-PWUserListOwners -UserListName 'MyUserList' -Owner 'AdminList' 
    -OwnerType 'userlist'
    
    Adds the user list 'AdminList' as an owner of the user list 'MyUserList' 
    using the long form 'userlist' for OwnerType. This is functionally 
    identical to Example 3.
    -------------------------- EXAMPLE 5 --------------------------
    
    $UserList = Get-PWUserList -UserListName 'MyUserList'
    Set-PWUserListOwners -UserList $UserList -Owner 'TestUser' -OwnerType 'u'
    
    Retrieves the user list object for 'MyUserList' and then adds the user 
    'TestUser' as an owner by passing the UserList object directly.
    -------------------------- EXAMPLE 6 --------------------------
    
    $UserList = Get-PWUserList -UserListName 'MyUserList'
    Set-PWUserListOwners -UserList $UserList -Owner 'AdminList' -OwnerType 
    'ul'
    
    Retrieves the user list object for 'MyUserList' and then adds the user 
    list 'AdminList' as an owner by passing the UserList object directly.
    -------------------------- EXAMPLE 7 --------------------------
    
    Get-PWUserList -UserListName 'MyUserList' | Set-PWUserListOwners -Owner 
    'TestUser' -OwnerType 'u'
    
    Pipes the user list object for 'MyUserList' to the cmdlet and adds the 
    user 'TestUser' as an owner.
    -------------------------- EXAMPLE 8 --------------------------
    
    Get-PWUserList -UserListName 'MyUserList' | Set-PWUserListOwners -Owner 
    'AdminList' -OwnerType 'userlist'
    
    Pipes the user list object for 'MyUserList' to the cmdlet and adds the 
    user list 'AdminList' as an owner.
    -------------------------- EXAMPLE 9 --------------------------
    
    Set-PWUserListOwners -UserListName 'MyUserList' -Owner 'TestUser' 
    -OwnerType 'u'
    Set-PWUserListOwners -UserListName 'MyUserList' -Owner 'TestUser' 
    -OwnerType 'u'
    
    Running the same command twice. The first command adds the user 
    'TestUser' as an owner and returns a success message. The second command 
    detects that 'TestUser' is already an owner and displays a warning 
    message.
    
RELATED LINKS

## Set-PWUserSetting


NAME
    Set-PWUserSetting
    
SYNOPSIS
    Sets the user setting or settings for a specified user, group or for the 
    all datasource's users.
    
    
SYNTAX
    Set-PWUserSetting -AllDatasourceUsers -ToDefaultValue 
    [<CommonParameters>]
    
    Set-PWUserSetting -AllDatasourceUsers -UserSettingCategory 
    <UserSetting+Category> [<CommonParameters>]
    
    Set-PWUserSetting -AllDatasourceUsers -ValueObjects <PSObject[]> 
    [<CommonParameters>]
    
    Set-PWUserSetting -DefaultValue -ValueObjects <PSObject[]> 
    [<CommonParameters>]
    
    Set-PWUserSetting -DefaultValue -UserSettingCategory 
    <UserSetting+Category> [<CommonParameters>]
    
    Set-PWUserSetting -GroupID <Int32> -ValueObjects <PSObject[]> 
    [<CommonParameters>]
    
    Set-PWUserSetting -GroupID <Int32> -ToDefaultValue [<CommonParameters>]
    
    Set-PWUserSetting -GroupID <Int32> -UserSettingCategory 
    <UserSetting+Category> [<CommonParameters>]
    
    Set-PWUserSetting -InputUser <User[]> -ToDefaultValue 
    [<CommonParameters>]
    
    Set-PWUserSetting -InputUser <User[]> -UserSettingCategory 
    <UserSetting+Category> [<CommonParameters>]
    
    Set-PWUserSetting -InputUser <User[]> -ValueObjects <PSObject[]> 
    [<CommonParameters>]
    
    Set-PWUserSetting -ToDefaultValue -UserID <Int32> [<CommonParameters>]
    
    Set-PWUserSetting -UserID <Int32> -UserSettingCategory 
    <UserSetting+Category> [<CommonParameters>]
    
    Set-PWUserSetting -UserID <Int32> -ValueObjects <PSObject[]> 
    [<CommonParameters>]
    
    
DESCRIPTION
    The Set-PWUserSetting cmdlet sets the setting value or values for a 
    given user, group, datasource default or for all datasource's users.
    

PARAMETERS
    -AllDatasourceUsers [<SwitchParameter>]
        Set the settings for all active datasource's users.
        
        Required?                    true
        Position?                    named
        Default value                False
        Accept pipeline input?       False
        Accept wildcard characters?  false
        
    -DefaultValue [<SwitchParameter>]
        Use this to change the server default value.
        
        Required?                    true
        Position?                    named
        Default value                False
        Accept pipeline input?       False
        Accept wildcard characters?  false
        
    -GroupID <Int32>
        The ID of the group whose user’s settings should be set.
        
        Required?                    true
        Position?                    named
        Default value                None
        Accept pipeline input?       True (ByPropertyName)
        Accept wildcard characters?  false
        
    -InputUser <User[]>
        The user object or objects whose settings should be set.
        
        Required?                    true
        Position?                    named
        Default value                None
        Accept pipeline input?       True (ByValue)
        Accept wildcard characters?  false
        
    -ToDefaultValue [<SwitchParameter>]
        Use this to set the setting to the currently configured server 
        default value.
        
        Required?                    true
        Position?                    named
        Default value                False
        Accept pipeline input?       False
        Accept wildcard characters?  false
        
    -UserID <Int32>
        The ID of the user whose settings should be set.
        
        Required?                    true
        Position?                    named
        Default value                None
        Accept pipeline input?       True (ByPropertyName)
        Accept wildcard characters?  false
        
    -UserSettingCategory <UserSetting+Category>
        The category (group) of the setting to set.
        
        Required?                    true
        Position?                    named
        Default value                None
        Accept pipeline input?       True (ByPropertyName)
        Accept wildcard characters?  false
        
    -ValueObjects <PSObject[]>
        Use this when importing settings (from a *.csv file for example).
        
        Required?                    true
        Position?                    named
        Default value                None
        Accept pipeline input?       False
        Accept wildcard characters?  false
        
    <CommonParameters>
        This cmdlet supports the common parameters: Verbose, Debug,
        ErrorAction, ErrorVariable, WarningAction, WarningVariable,
        OutBuffer, PipelineVariable, and OutVariable. For more information, 
    see 
        about_CommonParameters 
    (https:/go.microsoft.com/fwlink/?LinkID=113216). 
    
INPUTS
    
OUTPUTS
    
NOTES
    
    
        This cmdlet requires an open administrative ProjectWise Connection 
        in the current thread. You can open an administrative Connection 
        using the Open-PWConnection cmdlet and setting the -Admin flag.
    
    -------------------------- Example 1 --------------------------
    
    Set-PWUserSetting -UserID 2 -UserSettingCategory Admin -UserSettingName 
    Storage_CanCreate -UserSettingValue 1
    
    Set the "Storage_CanCreate" value to TRUE for the user with ID = 2.
    -------------------------- Example 2 --------------------------
    
    Import-Csv "c:\userSettings.txt" | Set-PWUserSetting -UserID 2
    
    or
    
    Set-PWUserSetting -UserID 2 -ValueObjects (Import-Csv 
    "c:\userSettings.txt")
    
    Set user (with ID = 2) settings from file.
    -------------------------- Example 3 --------------------------
    
    Set-PWUserSetting -UserID 2 -ToDefaultValue
    
    Set all user (with ID = 2) settings to default values.
    -------------------------- Example 4 --------------------------
    
    Set-PWUserSetting -AllDatasourceUsers -ToDefaultValue
    
    Set the settings for all active datasource’s users to default values.
    -------------------------- Example 5 --------------------------
    
    $user = Get-PWUser -UserName pwadmin
    Set-PWUserSetting -InputUser $user -UserSettingCategory Admin 
    -UserSettingName Storage_CanCreate -UserSettingValue 1
    
    Set the "Storage_CanCreate" value to TRUE for the user object(s) passed 
    in through the InputUser parameter.
    
RELATED LINKS
    Get-PWUserSetting

## Set-PWUserWorkingDirectory


NAME
    Set-PWUserWorkingDirectory
    
SYNOPSIS
    Adds a working directory for the user.
    
    
SYNTAX
    Set-PWUserWorkingDirectory [-WorkingFolder] <string> [-UserName 
    <string[]>] [<CommonParameters>]
    
    Set-PWUserWorkingDirectory [-WorkingFolder] <string> [-UserId <int[]>] 
    [<CommonParameters>]
    
    Set-PWUserWorkingDirectory [[-InputUser] <User[]>] [-WorkingFolder] 
    <string> [<CommonParameters>]
    
    
DESCRIPTION
    Sets the ProjectWise working directory for a selected user. Select the 
    user by name, ID, or user object and provide a local folder path.
    

PARAMETERS
    -UserName <string[]>
        List of user names to process.
        
        Required?                    false
        Position?                    named
        Default value                None
        Accept pipeline input?       True (ByValue, ByPropertyName)
        Accept wildcard characters?  false
        
    -UserId <int[]>
        List of user ids to add.
        
        Required?                    false
        Position?                    named
        Default value                None
        Accept pipeline input?       True (ByValue, ByPropertyName)
        Accept wildcard characters?  false
        
    -InputUser <User[]>
        Array of user objects to add.
        
        Required?                    false
        Position?                    0
        Default value                None
        Accept pipeline input?       True (ByValue)
        Accept wildcard characters?  false
        
    -WorkingFolder <string>
        Working folder name. Will create if does not exist.
        
        Required?                    true
        Position?                    1
        Default value                None
        Accept pipeline input?       True (ByValue)
        Accept wildcard characters?  false
        
    <CommonParameters>
        This cmdlet supports the common parameters: Verbose, Debug,
        ErrorAction, ErrorVariable, WarningAction, WarningVariable,
        OutBuffer, PipelineVariable, and OutVariable. For more information, 
    see 
        about_CommonParameters 
    (https:/go.microsoft.com/fwlink/?LinkID=113216). 
    
INPUTS
    System.String[]
        List of user names to process.
    
    System.Int32[]
        List of user ids to add.
    
    PWPS_DAB.Common.Users.User[]
        Array of user objects to add.
    
    System.String
        Working folder name. Will create if does not exist.
    
    
OUTPUTS
    
NOTES
    
    
        
    
    -------------------------- EXAMPLE 1 --------------------------
    
    #Set working directory for a user
    Set-PWUserWorkingDirectory -UserName pwadmin -WorkingDirectory 
    'c:\pwwork\ds1\pwadmin'
    #Set working directory for an array of users
    $users = Get-PWUsersByMatch xadmin13, xadmin14
    foreach ($user in $users)
    {
    Set-PWUserAdminRights $user -Verbose
    Set-PWUserWorkingDirectory $user -WorkingFolder("c:\pwwork\pwv8i\" + 
    $user.Name) -Verbose
    }

## Set-PWWorkAreaConnectionDisabledOIDC


NAME
    Set-PWWorkAreaConnectionDisabledOIDC
    
SYNOPSIS
    Enables or disables a Work Area connection.
    
    
SYNTAX
    Set-PWWorkAreaConnectionDisabledOIDC [-OIDCToken] <OIDCAuthToken> 
    [[-ConnectedProjectId] <string>] [[-WorkAreaConnectionId] <string>] 
    [[-Enable]] [[-PreserveName]] [<CommonParameters>]
    
    
DESCRIPTION
    Disables a Work Area connection in a Bentley-connected project. Use the 
    Enable switch to enable the connection instead.
    
    Provide an OIDC token, a connected project ID, and a Work Area 
    connection ID.
    

PARAMETERS
    -OIDCToken <OIDCAuthToken>
        Pass access_token part of OIDC Token returned from Get-OIDCToken 
        cmdlet. Authority https://ims.bentley.com. Required.
        
        Required?                    true
        Position?                    0
        Default value                None
        Accept pipeline input?       False
        Accept wildcard characters?  false
        
    -ConnectedProjectId <string>
        Connected/PW365 Project Id.
        
        Required?                    false
        Position?                    1
        Default value                None
        Accept pipeline input?       False
        Accept wildcard characters?  false
        
    -WorkAreaConnectionId <string>
        Work Area Connection Id.
        
        Required?                    false
        Position?                    2
        Default value                None
        Accept pipeline input?       False
        Accept wildcard characters?  false
        
    -Enable [<SwitchParameter>]
        Enable instead of disable.
        
        Required?                    false
        Position?                    3
        Default value                False
        Accept pipeline input?       False
        Accept wildcard characters?  false
        
    -PreserveName [<SwitchParameter>]
        When disabling, probably really want to set to a new, random name in 
        order to eliminate conflicts.
        
        Required?                    false
        Position?                    4
        Default value                False
        Accept pipeline input?       False
        Accept wildcard characters?  false
        
    <CommonParameters>
        This cmdlet supports the common parameters: Verbose, Debug,
        ErrorAction, ErrorVariable, WarningAction, WarningVariable,
        OutBuffer, PipelineVariable, and OutVariable. For more information, 
    see 
        about_CommonParameters 
    (https:/go.microsoft.com/fwlink/?LinkID=113216). 
    
INPUTS
    
OUTPUTS
    
NOTES
    
    
        
    
    -------------------------- EXAMPLE 1 --------------------------
    
    $oidcToken = Get-OIDCToken -Authority https://ims.bentley.com
    $wacs = Get-PWWorkAreaConnectionsOIDC -ConnectedProjectIds 
    '8136211F-C83B-4388-A59C-1D657734CD4F' -OIDCToken $oidcToken 
    Set-PWWorkAreaConnectionDisabledOIDC 
    '8136211F-C83B-4388-A59C-1D657734CD4F' -WorkAreaConnectionId $wacs[0].id 
    -OIDCToken $oidcToken

## Set-PWWorkAreaResource


NAME
    Set-PWWorkAreaResource
    
SYNOPSIS
    Sets or modifies resource assignments for a specified Work Area.
    
    
SYNTAX
    Set-PWWorkAreaResource [[-InputFolder] <ProjectWiseFolder[]>] 
    [-ResourceType] {application | department | environment | storagearea | 
    view | workflow | workspaceprofile | renditionprofile} 
    [[-ResourceMemberName] <String>] [-Inherit] [-None] [<CommonParameters>]
    
    Set-PWWorkAreaResource [-FolderPath] <String> [-ResourceType] 
    {application | department | environment | storagearea | view | workflow 
    | workspaceprofile | renditionprofile} [[-ResourceMemberName] <String>] 
    [-Inherit] [-None] [<CommonParameters>]
    
    
DESCRIPTION
    The Set-PWWorkAreaResource cmdlet is used to manipulate resource 
    assignments for a Work Area. This cmdlet allows administrators to manage 
    how resources are assigned to a Work Area by:
    
    - Setting a resource type to inherit from the parent folder using the 
    -Inherit switch
    
    - Setting a resource type to none (no resources available) using the 
    -None switch
    
    - Adding a specific resource member to the Work Area using the 
    -ResourceMemberName parameter
    
    
    Only one of these options can be used at a time. If multiple options are 
    specified, the cmdlet will display a warning and exit without making 
    changes.
    
    The cmdlet supports two ways to specify the target Work Area: by folder 
    path using -FolderPath, or by passing ProjectWiseFolder objects via 
    -InputFolder or pipeline input.
    Note: The 'workspaceprofile' resource type is currently not implemented 
    and will not function.
    
    

PARAMETERS
    -InputFolder <ProjectWiseFolder[]>
        Specifies an array of ProjectWiseFolder objects representing the 
        Work Areas to modify. This parameter accepts pipeline input.
        
        Required?                    false
        Position?                    0
        Default value                None
        Accept pipeline input?       True (ByValue)
        Accept wildcard characters?  false
        
    -FolderPath <String>
        Specifies the folder path of the Work Area to modify.
        
        Required?                    true
        Position?                    0
        Default value                None
        Accept pipeline input?       True (ByValue)
        Accept wildcard characters?  false
        
    -ResourceType <String>
        Specifies the resource type to set or modify.
        
        Valid values are: - application
        
        - department
        
        - environment
        
        - storagearea
        
        - view
        
        - workflow
        
        - workspaceprofile (not implemented)
        
        - renditionprofile
        
        Required?                    true
        Position?                    1
        Default value                None
        Accept pipeline input?       False
        Accept wildcard characters?  false
        
    -ResourceMemberName <String>
        Specifies the name of the resource member to add to the Work Area. 
        The name must match an existing member of the specified resource 
        type. The matching is case-insensitive.
        
        This parameter cannot be used together with -Inherit or -None.
        
        Required?                    false
        Position?                    2
        Default value                None
        Accept pipeline input?       False
        Accept wildcard characters?  false
        
    -Inherit [<SwitchParameter>]
        When specified, sets the resource type to inherit from the parent 
        folder. This removes any explicit resource assignments for the 
        specified resource type.
        
        This parameter cannot be used together with -ResourceMemberName or 
        -None.
        
        Required?                    false
        Position?                    named
        Default value                False
        Accept pipeline input?       False
        Accept wildcard characters?  false
        
    -None [<SwitchParameter>]
        When specified, sets the resource type to none, meaning no resources 
        of that type will be available for the Work Area.
        
        This parameter cannot be used together with -ResourceMemberName or 
        -Inherit.
        
        Required?                    false
        Position?                    named
        Default value                False
        Accept pipeline input?       False
        Accept wildcard characters?  false
        
    <CommonParameters>
        This cmdlet supports the common parameters: Verbose, Debug,
        ErrorAction, ErrorVariable, WarningAction, WarningVariable,
        OutBuffer, PipelineVariable, and OutVariable. For more information, 
    see 
        about_CommonParameters 
    (https:/go.microsoft.com/fwlink/?LinkID=113216). 
    
INPUTS
    PWPS_DAB.CommonTypes+ProjectWiseFolder[]
        An array of ProjectWiseFolder objects representing the Work Areas to 
        modify. This parameter accepts pipeline input.
    
    System.String
        The folder path of the Work Area to modify.
    
    
OUTPUTS
    None
        This cmdlet does not return any output. Use the -Verbose parameter 
        to see detailed progress messages.
    
    
NOTES
    
    
        - The specified folder must be a Work Area. If it is not, the cmdlet 
        displays a warning and continues to the next folder (if processing 
        multiple folders).
        
        - Only one of -Inherit, -None, or -ResourceMemberName can be 
        specified at a time. If multiple options are specified, the cmdlet 
        displays a warning and exits without making changes.
        
        - If the specified resource member already exists for the Work Area, 
        the cmdlet displays a warning and continues.
        
        - If an invalid resource member name is specified, the cmdlet 
        displays an error and continues to the next folder.
        
        - The ResourceMemberName parameter matching is case-insensitive.
        
        - The 'workspaceprofile' resource type is listed as a valid option 
        but is currently not implemented.
        
        - When adding a resource member to a Work Area that was previously 
        set to "none", the cmdlet automatically removes the "none" setting.
    
    - EXAMPLE 1: Set a resource type to inherit using folder path -
    
    Set-PWWorkAreaResource -FolderPath 'Projects\Building Design' 
    -ResourceType 'application' -Inherit
    
    Sets the 'application' resource type to inherit from the parent folder 
    for the specified Work Area.
    --- EXAMPLE 2: Set a resource type to none using folder path ---
    
    Set-PWWorkAreaResource -FolderPath 'Projects\Civil Engineering' 
    -ResourceType 'department' -None
    
    Sets the 'department' resource type to none, meaning no departments will 
    be available for the specified Work Area.
    -------- EXAMPLE 3: Add an application resource member --------
    
    Set-PWWorkAreaResource -FolderPath 'Projects\Infrastructure' 
    -ResourceType 'application' -ResourceMemberName 'MicroStation'
    
    Adds the 'MicroStation' application to the Work Area's application 
    resources.
    EXAMPLE 4: Add a department resource member with verbose output
    
    Set-PWWorkAreaResource -FolderPath 'Projects\Structural' -ResourceType 
    'department' -ResourceMemberName 'Engineering' -Verbose
    
    Adds the 'Engineering' department to the Work Area and displays verbose 
    messages during execution.
    -------- EXAMPLE 5: Add an environment resource member --------
    
    Set-PWWorkAreaResource -FolderPath 'Projects\MEP' -ResourceType 
    'environment' -ResourceMemberName 'Production'
    
    Adds the 'Production' environment to the Work Area's environment 
    resources.
    -------- EXAMPLE 6: Add a storage area resource member --------
    
    Set-PWWorkAreaResource -FolderPath 'Projects\Architectural' 
    -ResourceType 'storagearea' -ResourceMemberName 'PrimaryStorage'
    
    Adds the 'PrimaryStorage' storage area to the Work Area's storage area 
    resources.
    ------------ EXAMPLE 7: Add a view resource member ------------
    
    Set-PWWorkAreaResource -FolderPath 'Projects\Documentation' 
    -ResourceType 'view' -ResourceMemberName 'StandardView'
    
    Adds the 'StandardView' view to the Work Area's view resources.
    ---------- EXAMPLE 8: Add a workflow resource member ----------
    
    Set-PWWorkAreaResource -FolderPath 'Projects\Highway Design' 
    -ResourceType 'workflow' -ResourceMemberName 'ApprovalWorkflow'
    
    Adds the 'ApprovalWorkflow' workflow to the Work Area's workflow 
    resources.
    ------ EXAMPLE 9: Add a rendition profile resource member ------
    
    Set-PWWorkAreaResource -FolderPath 'Projects\Publishing' -ResourceType 
    'renditionprofile' -ResourceMemberName 'PDFProfile'
    
    Adds the 'PDFProfile' rendition profile to the Work Area's rendition 
    profile resources.
    ----- EXAMPLE 10: Set resource using InputFolder parameter -----
    
    $folder = Get-PWFolders -FolderPath 'Projects\Building Design'
    Set-PWWorkAreaResource -InputFolder $folder -ResourceType 'application' 
    -ResourceMemberName 'OpenRoads'
    
    Adds the 'OpenRoads' application to the Work Area using the InputFolder 
    parameter.
    -------- EXAMPLE 11: Set resource using pipeline input --------
    
    Get-PWFolders -FolderPath 'Projects\Civil Engineering' | 
    Set-PWWorkAreaResource -ResourceType 'department' -ResourceMemberName 
    'CivilDept'
    
    Pipes a Work Area folder to the cmdlet and adds the 'CivilDept' 
    department resource member.
    EXAMPLE 12: Set resource type to inherit for multiple Work Areas
    
    $folders = Get-PWFolders -FolderPath 'Projects\Civil%'
    foreach ($folder in $folders) {
        Set-PWWorkAreaResource -InputFolder $folder -ResourceType 
    'environment' -Inherit -Verbose
    }
    
    Iterates through multiple Work Areas and sets the environment resource 
    type to inherit for each.
    EXAMPLE 13: Set resource type to none for multiple Work Areas
    
    $folders = Get-PWFolders -FolderPath 'Projects\Archive%'
    foreach ($folder in $folders) {
        Set-PWWorkAreaResource -InputFolder $folder -ResourceType 'workflow' 
    -None -Verbose
    }
    
    Iterates through multiple Work Areas and sets the workflow resource type 
    to none for each.
    
RELATED LINKS

## Show-PWDocumentSelectionDialog


NAME
    Show-PWDocumentSelectionDialog
    
SYNOPSIS
    Opens a dialog to select ProjectWise documents.
    
    
SYNTAX
    Show-PWDocumentSelectionDialog [[-InitialFolderPath] <string>] 
    [[-FileExtension] <string>] [[-DialogTitle] <string>] 
    [<CommonParameters>]
    
    
DESCRIPTION
    Opens a ProjectWise dialog for selecting one or more documents.
    
    Use InitialFolderPath to choose the starting folder, FileExtension to 
    limit file types, and DialogTitle to set the window title.
    
    Requires an active ProjectWise connection and an interactive desktop 
    session.
    

PARAMETERS
    -InitialFolderPath <string>
        Initial folder path (Optional).
        
        Required?                    false
        Position?                    0
        Default value                None
        Accept pipeline input?       False
        Accept wildcard characters?  false
        
    -FileExtension <string>
        File extension filter (Optional).
        
        Required?                    false
        Position?                    1
        Default value                None
        Accept pipeline input?       False
        Accept wildcard characters?  false
        
    -DialogTitle <string>
        Dialog title (Optional).
        
        Required?                    false
        Position?                    2
        Default value                Select documents for PowerShell
        Accept pipeline input?       False
        Accept wildcard characters?  false
        
    <CommonParameters>
        This cmdlet supports the common parameters: Verbose, Debug,
        ErrorAction, ErrorVariable, WarningAction, WarningVariable,
        OutBuffer, PipelineVariable, and OutVariable. For more information, 
    see 
        about_CommonParameters 
    (https:/go.microsoft.com/fwlink/?LinkID=113216). 
    
INPUTS
    
OUTPUTS
    PWPS_DAB.CommonTypes+ProjectWiseDocument[]
        
    
    
NOTES
    
    
        
    
    -------------------------- EXAMPLE 1 --------------------------
    
    $docs = Show-PWDocumentSelectionDialog 'UDOT\Kofax\GAX\2013' 
    -FileExtension tif
    Set-PWDocumentFinalStatus $docs
    
    
    
RELATED LINKS

## Undo-PWLogin


NAME
    Undo-PWLogin
    
SYNOPSIS
    Logout cmdlet native to this module.
    
    
SYNTAX
    Undo-PWLogin [<CommonParameters>]
    
    
DESCRIPTION
    The `Undo-PWLogin` cmdlet logs out of the currently active ProjectWise 
    datasource session. It terminates the connection to the datasource, 
    removes the session from the current session tracking, and clears the 
    session environment variable. If there is an active datasource, the 
    cmdlet logs out and returns `True` to indicate success. If no active 
    datasource is found, the cmdlet performs no action.
    

PARAMETERS
    <CommonParameters>
        This cmdlet supports the common parameters: Verbose, Debug,
        ErrorAction, ErrorVariable, WarningAction, WarningVariable,
        OutBuffer, PipelineVariable, and OutVariable. For more information, 
    see 
        about_CommonParameters 
    (https:/go.microsoft.com/fwlink/?LinkID=113216). 
    
INPUTS
    
OUTPUTS
    
NOTES
    
    
        
    
    -------------------------- EXAMPLE 1 --------------------------
    
    Regular logout
    Undo-PWLogin
    
    
    
RELATED LINKS

## Update-CloudProjectWorkAreaConnections


NAME
    Update-CloudProjectWorkAreaConnections
    
SYNOPSIS
    Updates settings for a Work Area connection.
    
    
SYNTAX
    Update-CloudProjectWorkAreaConnections [[-InputFolder] 
    <ProjectWiseFolder[]>] [[-CloudProjectId] <string>] [-OIDCToken] 
    <OIDCAuthToken> [[-APIVersion] <string>] [-ConnectionId] <string> 
    [[-SetAsPrimary]] [[-SetAutoUserSync]] [[-SetDriveSync]] 
    [[-SetOnAccessUserSync]] [<CommonParameters>]
    
    
DESCRIPTION
    Updates the primary connection and synchronization settings for one Work 
    Area connection in a Bentley Infrastructure Cloud project.
    
    Provide a Cloud project ID or a connected ProjectWise Work Area, an OIDC 
    token and the connection ID. Use switches to set the primary connection 
    and synchronization options.
    

PARAMETERS
    -InputFolder <ProjectWiseFolder[]>
        Array of ProjectWise folders from pipeline. Work Area Connections 
        from the corresponding Bentley Infrastructure Cloud Projects will be 
        shown.
        
        Required?                    false
        Position?                    0
        Default value                PWPS_DAB.CommonTypes+ProjectWiseFolder[]
        Accept pipeline input?       True (ByValue)
        Accept wildcard characters?  false
        
    -CloudProjectId <string>
        Bentley Infrastructure Cloud Project ID to query
        
        Required?                    false
        Position?                    1
        Default value                None
        Accept pipeline input?       False
        Accept wildcard characters?  false
        
    -OIDCToken <OIDCAuthToken>
        Token from Get-OIDCToken -Authority https://ims.bentley.com
        
        Required?                    true
        Position?                    2
        Default value                None
        Accept pipeline input?       False
        Accept wildcard characters?  false
        
    -APIVersion <string>
        
        
        Required?                    false
        Position?                    3
        Default value                V1
        Accept pipeline input?       False
        Accept wildcard characters?  false
        
    -ConnectionId <string>
        Connection ID required. Use Get-CloudProjectWorkAreaConnections to 
        get IDs.
        
        Required?                    true
        Position?                    4
        Default value                None
        Accept pipeline input?       False
        Accept wildcard characters?  false
        
    -SetAsPrimary [<SwitchParameter>]
        Set this WAC to be the Primary Connection. Will cause the previous 
        primary connection to be unset.
        
        Required?                    false
        Position?                    5
        Default value                False
        Accept pipeline input?       False
        Accept wildcard characters?  false
        
    -SetAutoUserSync [<SwitchParameter>]
        Set this WAC to be Automatic User Sync. Will cause the previous auto 
        user sync connection to be unset.
        
        Required?                    false
        Position?                    6
        Default value                False
        Accept pipeline input?       False
        Accept wildcard characters?  false
        
    -SetDriveSync [<SwitchParameter>]
        Set this WAC to be Drive Synced. Will cause the previous Drive sync 
        connection to be unset.
        
        Required?                    false
        Position?                    7
        Default value                False
        Accept pipeline input?       False
        Accept wildcard characters?  false
        
    -SetOnAccessUserSync [<SwitchParameter>]
        Set this WAC to be On Access Synced.
        
        Required?                    false
        Position?                    8
        Default value                False
        Accept pipeline input?       False
        Accept wildcard characters?  false
        
    <CommonParameters>
        This cmdlet supports the common parameters: Verbose, Debug,
        ErrorAction, ErrorVariable, WarningAction, WarningVariable,
        OutBuffer, PipelineVariable, and OutVariable. For more information, 
    see 
        about_CommonParameters 
    (https:/go.microsoft.com/fwlink/?LinkID=113216). 
    
INPUTS
    PWPS_DAB.CommonTypes+ProjectWiseFolder[]
        Array of ProjectWise folders from pipeline. Work Area Connections 
        from the corresponding Bentley Infrastructure Cloud Projects will be 
        shown.
    
    
OUTPUTS
    
NOTES
    
    
        
    
    -------------------------- EXAMPLE 1 --------------------------
    
    $oidcToken = Get-OIDCToken -Authority https://ims.bentley.com
    $folder = Get-PWFolderByURN 'URNtoWorkAreaWithWorkAreaConnections'
    $wacs = Get-CloudProjectWorkAreaConnections $folder -OIDCToken $oidcToken
    Update-CloudProjectWorkAreaConnections $folder -OIDCToken $oidcToken 
    -ConnectionId $wacs[0].Id -SetDriveSync
    
    
    
RELATED LINKS

## Update-PWCelLibraryExpression


NAME
    Update-PWCelLibraryExpression
    
SYNOPSIS
    Updates an existing CEL Library Expression.
    
    
SYNTAX
    Update-PWCelLibraryExpression -Name <String> [-Expression <String>] 
    [-Description <String>] [<CommonParameters>]
    
    
DESCRIPTION
    The Update-PWCelLibraryExpression cmdlet updates an existing CEL library 
    expression in the ProjectWise datasource.
    
    Only the -Name parameter is required. If -Expression or -Description are 
    not specified, the existing values are preserved.
    

PARAMETERS
    -Name <String>
        The name of the CEL library expression to update.
        
        Required?                    true
        Position?                    named
        Default value                None
        Accept pipeline input?       False
        Accept wildcard characters?  false
        
    -Expression <String>
        The new CEL expression content to replace the existing expression. 
        If not specified, the existing expression content is preserved.
        
        Required?                    false
        Position?                    named
        Default value                None
        Accept pipeline input?       False
        Accept wildcard characters?  false
        
    -Description <String>
        The new description for the CEL library expression. If not 
        specified, the existing description is preserved.
        
        Required?                    false
        Position?                    named
        Default value                None
        Accept pipeline input?       False
        Accept wildcard characters?  false
        
    <CommonParameters>
        This cmdlet supports the common parameters: Verbose, Debug,
        ErrorAction, ErrorVariable, WarningAction, WarningVariable,
        OutBuffer, PipelineVariable, and OutVariable. For more information, 
    see 
        about_CommonParameters 
    (https:/go.microsoft.com/fwlink/?LinkID=113216). 
    
INPUTS
    
OUTPUTS
    
NOTES
    
    
        
    
    -------- Example 1: Update only the expression content --------
    
    PS C:\> Update-PWCelLibraryExpression -Name "MyExpression" -Expression 
    "'new expression content'"
    
    Updates only the expression content of the CEL library expression named 
    "MyExpression". The existing description is preserved.
    ------------ Example 2: Update only the description ------------
    
    PS C:\> Update-PWCelLibraryExpression -Name "MyExpression" -Description 
    "Updated description"
    
    Updates only the description of the CEL library expression named 
    "MyExpression". The existing expression content is preserved.
    ------ Example 3: Update both expression and description ------
    
    PS C:\> Update-PWCelLibraryExpression -Name "MyExpression" -Expression 
    "'new expression'" -Description "New description"
    
    Updates both the expression content and description of the CEL library 
    expression named "MyExpression".
    
RELATED LINKS

## Update-PWDatasourceConfigEntry


NAME
    Update-PWDatasourceConfigEntry
    
SYNOPSIS
    Updates a datasource config entry in the specified host's dmskrnl.cfg.
    
    
SYNTAX
    Update-PWDatasourceConfigEntry [-HostName] <String> [-DSConfigEntry] 
    <DatasourceConfigEntry> [[-DBPassword] <Object>] [<CommonParameters>]
    
    
DESCRIPTION
    The Update-PWDatasourceConfigEntry cmdlet updates the datasource config 
    entry of the given DBID in the specified host's dmskrnl.cfg.
    

PARAMETERS
    -DBPassword <Object>
        If set then specifies new database password to set for the 
        datasource config entry in the specified host's dmskrnl.cfg.
        
        Required?                    false
        Position?                    2
        Default value                None
        Accept pipeline input?       False
        Accept wildcard characters?  false
        
    -DSConfigEntry <DatasourceConfigEntry>
        Specifies the DatasourceConfigEntry object with the data (except 
        database password) to update datasource config entry in the 
        specified host's dmskrnl.cfg.
        
        Required?                    true
        Position?                    1
        Default value                None
        Accept pipeline input?       True (ByPropertyName)
        Accept wildcard characters?  false
        
    -HostName <String>
        Specifies the hostname with ProjectWise Server configured.
        
        Required?                    true
        Position?                    0
        Default value                None
        Accept pipeline input?       True (ByPropertyName)
        Accept wildcard characters?  false
        
    <CommonParameters>
        This cmdlet supports the common parameters: Verbose, Debug,
        ErrorAction, ErrorVariable, WarningAction, WarningVariable,
        OutBuffer, PipelineVariable, and OutVariable. For more information, 
    see 
        about_CommonParameters 
    (https:/go.microsoft.com/fwlink/?LinkID=113216). 
    
INPUTS
    
OUTPUTS
    
NOTES
    
    
        
    
    -------------------------- Example 1 --------------------------
    
    $dsEntry = Get-PWDatasourceConfigEntry HOSTNAME db1;
    $dsEntry.DisplayName = "New Display Name";
    $dsEntry.Description = "New Description";
    $dsEntry.InterfaceType = ODBC;
    $dsEntry.Type = SQLSERVER;
    $dsEntry.Name = "ODBC System Data Source Name";
    $dsEntry.DBUserName = "Database User Name";
    $dsEntry.SSO = 0;
    $dsEntry.STS = 0;
    Update-PWDatasourceConfigEntry -HostName HOSTNAME -DSConfigEntry $dsEntry
    
    Updates all available fields for datasource config entry.
    
    DBID                 : db1 Description          : New Description Type   
                  : SQLServer DisplayName          : New Display Name 
    InterfaceType        : ODBC Name                 : ODBC System Data 
    Source Name DBUserName           : Database User Name SSO                
      : 0 STS                  : 0 ImsServiceIdentities : {}
    -------------------------- Example 2 --------------------------
    
    $dsEntry = Get-PWDatasourceConfigEntry HOSTNAME db1;
    Update-PWDatasourceConfigEntry HOSTNAME $dsEntry 'SQL Database User 
    Password'
    
    Sets new database password for database user in the datasource config 
    entry.
    
    DBID                 : db1 Description          : New Description Type   
                  : SQLServer DisplayName          : New Display Name 
    InterfaceType        : ODBC Name                 : ODBC System Data 
    Source Name DBUserName           : Database User Name SSO                
      : 0 STS                  : 0 ImsServiceIdentities : {}
    -------------------------- Example 3 --------------------------
    
    $dsEntry = Get-PWDatasourceConfigEntry HOSTNAME db1;
    Update-PWDatasourceConfigEntry HOSTNAME $dsEntry
    
    On trying to update datasource config entry with the object without 
    changes and no new password specified shows the warning in the output.
    
    WARNING: Nothing to do - given DatasourceConfigEntry object matches 
    current values!
    -------------------------- Example 4 --------------------------
    
    $dsEntry = Get-PWDatasourceConfigEntry HOSTNAME;
    $imsSi1 = New-PWImsServiceIdentity -IdentityID "IMS1" -IdentityProvider 
    "https://ims.bentley.com/" -IdentityName "SomeName" -IdentityKey 
    "SomeKey";
    $dsEntry.ImsServiceIdentities.Add($imsSi1);
    Update-PWDatasourceConfigEntry HOSTNAME $dsEntry
    
    Configure (add) a new IMS Service Identity for the active PW datasource.
    
    DBID                 : db1 Description          : New Description Type   
                  : SQLServer DisplayName          : New Display Name 
    InterfaceType        : ODBC Name                 : ODBC System Data 
    Source Name DBUserName           : Database User Name SSO                
      : 0 STS                  : 0 ImsServiceIdentities : {IMS1}
    
RELATED LINKS
    Get-PWDatasourceConfigEntry 
    Initialize-PWDatasourceConfigEntry 
    Remove-PWDatasourceConfigEntry

## Update-PWDocumentAttributes


NAME

SYNOPSIS
    Updates or creates a new document attribute record.
    
    
SYNTAX
    Update-PWDocumentAttributes [-InputDocuments] <ProjectWiseDocument[]> 
    [-Attributes] <Hashtable> [[-ReturnBoolean]] [<CommonParameters>]
    
    
DESCRIPTION
    Takes an array of document objects from the pipeline and a hashtable of 
    attribute values and updates or creates a document attribute record.
    

PARAMETERS
    -InputDocuments <ProjectWiseDocument[]>
        Array of document objects from the pipeline.
        
        Required?                    true
        Position?                    0
        Default value                None
        Accept pipeline input?       True (ByValue)
        Accept wildcard characters?  false
        
    -Attributes <Hashtable>
        Hashtable of attributes. Hashtable in PowerShell looks like this 
        @{Name = 'Value'; Name2 = 'Value2' }
        
        Required?                    true
        Position?                    1
        Default value                None
        Accept pipeline input?       False
        Accept wildcard characters?  false
        
    -ReturnBoolean [<SwitchParameter>]
        Use this switch to return a boolean value indicating if all the 
        passed documents were updated successfully. Default is to pass 
        updated documents out through the pipeline.
        
        Required?                    false
        Position?                    2
        Default value                False
        Accept pipeline input?       False
        Accept wildcard characters?  false
        
    <CommonParameters>
        This cmdlet supports the common parameters: Verbose, Debug,
        ErrorAction, ErrorVariable, WarningAction, WarningVariable,
        OutBuffer, PipelineVariable, and OutVariable. For more information, 
    see 
        about_CommonParameters 
    (https:/go.microsoft.com/fwlink/?LinkID=113216). 
    
INPUTS
    PWPS_DAB.CommonTypes+ProjectWiseDocument[]
        Array of document objects from the pipeline.
    
    
OUTPUTS
    
NOTES
    
    
        
    
    -------------------------- EXAMPLE 1 --------------------------
    
    #Get the documents in the given path and update them with the listed 
    attributes
    Show-PWDocumentSelectionDialog | Update-PWDocumentAttributes -Attributes 
    @{Title = "New Title"; Dwg_no = "007"; SomeNumber = "555" }

## Update-PWDocumentFile


NAME
    Update-PWDocumentFile
    
SYNOPSIS
    Replaces or removes ProjectWise document files.
    
    
SYNTAX
    Update-PWDocumentFile [-InputDocuments] <ProjectWiseDocument[]> 
    [-NewFilePathName] <string> [[-NewFileName] <string>] [-RemoveFile] 
    [<CommonParameters>]
    
    Update-PWDocumentFile [-InputDocuments] <ProjectWiseDocument[]> 
    [-NewFilePathName] <string> [-KeepExistingFileName] [-RemoveFile] 
    [<CommonParameters>]
    
    
DESCRIPTION
    Replaces the file associated with the specified ProjectWise documents, 
    or removes the file and converts the documents to abstract documents.
    

PARAMETERS
    -InputDocuments <ProjectWiseDocument[]>
        Target document object(s) from pipeline.
        
        Required?                    true
        Position?                    0
        Default value                None
        Accept pipeline input?       True (ByValue)
        Accept wildcard characters?  false
        
    -NewFilePathName <string>
        Replacement file path.
        
        Required?                    true
        Position?                    1
        Default value                None
        Accept pipeline input?       False
        Accept wildcard characters?  false
        
    -NewFileName <string>
        New file name to apply.
        
        Required?                    false
        Position?                    2
        Default value                None
        Accept pipeline input?       False
        Accept wildcard characters?  false
        
    -KeepExistingFileName [<SwitchParameter>]
        When included, the existing file name will be used regardless of the 
        name of the specified file.
        
        Required?                    false
        Position?                    named
        Default value                False
        Accept pipeline input?       False
        Accept wildcard characters?  false
        
    -RemoveFile [<SwitchParameter>]
        If set, remove the existing file leaving the document as an abstract 
        document.
        
        Required?                    false
        Position?                    named
        Default value                False
        Accept pipeline input?       False
        Accept wildcard characters?  false
        
    <CommonParameters>
        This cmdlet supports the common parameters: Verbose, Debug,
        ErrorAction, ErrorVariable, WarningAction, WarningVariable,
        OutBuffer, PipelineVariable, and OutVariable. For more information, 
    see 
        about_CommonParameters 
    (https:/go.microsoft.com/fwlink/?LinkID=113216). 
    
INPUTS
    PWPS_DAB.CommonTypes+ProjectWiseDocument[]
        Target document object(s) from pipeline.
    
    
OUTPUTS
    
NOTES
    
    
        
    
    -------------------------- EXAMPLE 1 --------------------------
    
    #Example usage replacing physical file.
    The following will replace the existing file associated with the 
    ProjectWise document(s) The file name of the specified file will be used.
      Get-PWDocumentsBySearch -FolderPath "Examples" -DocumentName 
    "A111.dwg" -JustThisFolder -Verbose |
                    Update-PWDocumentFile -NewFilePathName 
    "D:\TEMP\Export\A111_Updated.dwg" -Verbose
    
    
    -------------------------- EXAMPLE 2 --------------------------
    
    #Example usage replacing physical file and renaming using document name.
    The following will replace the existing file associated with the 
    ProjectWise document(s) and rename using the new file name.
      Get-PWDocumentsBySearch -FolderPath "Examples" -DocumentName 
    "A111.dwg" -JustThisFolder -Verbose |
                    Update-PWDocumentFile -NewFilePathName 
    "D:\TEMP\Export\A111_Updated.dwg" -NewFileName "A111_UpdatedFile.dwg" 
    -Verbose
    
    
    -------------------------- EXAMPLE 3 --------------------------
    
    #Example usage replacing physical file and renaming with specified new 
    name.
    The following will replace the existing file associated with the 
    ProjectWise document(s) and keep the original associated file name.
      Get-PWDocumentsBySearch -FolderPath "Examples" -DocumentName 
    "A111.dwg" -JustThisFolder -Verbose |
                    Update-PWDocumentFile -NewFilePathName 
    "D:\TEMP\Export\A111_Updated.dwg" -KeepExistingFileName -Verbose
    
    
    
RELATED LINKS

## Update-PWDocumentProperties


NAME
    Update-PWDocumentProperties
    
SYNOPSIS
    Updates document general properties.
    
    
SYNTAX
    Update-PWDocumentProperties [-InputDocument] <ProjectWiseDocument[]> 
    [<CommonParameters>]
    
    
DESCRIPTION
    Takes an array of document objects from the pipeline and allows you to 
    reset the document name, description, file name or version string. 
    Update the properties you want to set.
    

PARAMETERS
    -InputDocument <ProjectWiseDocument[]>
        Array of document objects from pipeline to operate on (Required).
        
        Required?                    true
        Position?                    0
        Default value                None
        Accept pipeline input?       True (ByValue)
        Accept wildcard characters?  false
        
    <CommonParameters>
        This cmdlet supports the common parameters: Verbose, Debug,
        ErrorAction, ErrorVariable, WarningAction, WarningVariable,
        OutBuffer, PipelineVariable, and OutVariable. For more information, 
    see 
        about_CommonParameters 
    (https:/go.microsoft.com/fwlink/?LinkID=113216). 
    
INPUTS
    PWPS_DAB.CommonTypes+ProjectWiseDocument[]
        Array of document objects from pipeline to operate on (Required).
    
    
OUTPUTS
    
NOTES
    
    
        
    
    -------------------------- EXAMPLE 1 --------------------------
    
    #Get the documents in the given path and update them with the listed 
    attributes. Also supports changing name and filename, but, of course, 
    those must be unique in a folder.
     $docs = Get-PWDocumentsBySearch -FolderPath "Lend Lease\Dotted PDFs"
    foreach ($doc in $docs)
    {
        $doc.Description = "Changed in PowerShell 2"
        $doc.Version = "R06"
    }
    Update-PWDocumentProperties -InputDocument $docs
    Get-PWDocumentsBySearch -FolderPath "Lend Lease\Dotted PDFs" | Foreach 
    -MemberName Description
