## Update-PWFolderSecurity


NAME
    Update-PWFolderSecurity
    
SYNOPSIS
    Update-PWFolderSecurity is used to add user(s), group(s), or userlist(s) 
    access control to specified folders.
    
    
SYNTAX
    Update-PWFolderSecurity [-InputFolder] <ProjectWiseFolder[]> 
    [-MemberType] <string> [[-MemberName] <string[]>] [[-MemberAccess] 
    <string[]>] [-FolderSecurity] [-DocumentSecurity] [-WorkFlowName 
    <string>] [-StateName <string>] [-IncludeInheritance] 
    [<CommonParameters>]
    
    Update-PWFolderSecurity [-InputFolder] <ProjectWiseFolder[]> 
    [-MemberType] <string> [[-MemberName] <string[]>] [[-MemberAccessString] 
    <string>] [-FolderSecurity] [-DocumentSecurity] [-WorkFlowName <string>] 
    [-StateName <string>] [-IncludeInheritance] [<CommonParameters>]
    
    Update-PWFolderSecurity [-InputFolder] <ProjectWiseFolder[]> 
    [-MemberType] <string> [[-MemberName] <string[]>] [[-Role] <string>] 
    [-WorkFlowName <string>] [-StateName <string>] [-IncludeInheritance] 
    [<CommonParameters>]
    
    
DESCRIPTION
    The Membertype specifies whether access should be applied for a user, 
    group, or userlist.
    
    Valid entries:
    
    For users: "u", "user"
    
    For groups: "g", "group"
    
    For userlists: "ul", "userlist"
    
    For Everyone: "Everyone"
    
    The MemberAccess specifies the access control to be applied.
    
    Valid entries:
    
    fc - full control,
    
    cp - change permissions,
    
    c - create,
    
    d - delete,
    
    r - read,
    
    w - write,
    
    cw - change workflow,
    
    fr - file read,
    
    fw - file write,
    
    f - free,
    
    na - no access
    

PARAMETERS
    -InputFolder <ProjectWiseFolder[]>
        The InputFolders parameter contains the folder(s) to apply the 
        access control to.
        
        Required?                    true
        Position?                    0
        Default value                PWPS_DAB.CommonTypes+ProjectWiseFolder[]
        Accept pipeline input?       True (ByValue)
        Accept wildcard characters?  false
        
    -MemberType <string>
        The Membertype specifies whether access should be applied
        
        for a user, group, or userlist
        
        Valid entries are
        
        For users: "u", "User"
        
        For groups: "g", "Group"
        
        For userlists: "ul", "UserList"
        
        Required?                    true
        Position?                    1
        Default value                None
        Accept pipeline input?       False
        Accept wildcard characters?  false
        
    -MemberName <string[]>
        Name of user, group or userlist
        
        Required?                    false
        Position?                    2
        Default value                None
        Accept pipeline input?       False
        Accept wildcard characters?  false
        
    -MemberAccess <string[]>
        The MemberAccess specifies the access control to be applied.
        
        The available entries are:
        
        fc - full control,
        
        cp - change permissions,
        
        c - create,
        
        d - delete,
        
        r - read,
        
        w - write,
        
        cw - change workflow,
        
        fr - file read,
        
        fw - file write,
        
        f - free,
        
        na - no access
        
        Required?                    false
        Position?                    3
        Default value                None
        Accept pipeline input?       False
        Accept wildcard characters?  false
        
    -MemberAccessString <string>
        The MemberAccessString specifies an 11 character mask representing 
        the access control to be applied. This parameter is an alternative 
        to MemberAccess. Both may not be used together.
        
        Required?                    false
        Position?                    3
        Default value                None
        Accept pipeline input?       False
        Accept wildcard characters?  false
        
    -Role <string>
        The role name to be applied. Valid roles are:
        
        "Project Administrator"
        
        "Project Leader"
        
        "Team Member"
        
        "Project Observer"
        
        "No Access"
        
        "(Roles resolve to a specific set of rights on a project)"
        
        Required?                    false
        Position?                    4
        Default value                None
        Accept pipeline input?       False
        Accept wildcard characters?  false
        
    -FolderSecurity [<SwitchParameter>]
        Apply Folder Security.
        
        Required?                    false
        Position?                    named
        Default value                False
        Accept pipeline input?       False
        Accept wildcard characters?  false
        
    -DocumentSecurity [<SwitchParameter>]
        Apply Document Security.
        
        Required?                    false
        Position?                    named
        Default value                False
        Accept pipeline input?       False
        Accept wildcard characters?  false
        
    -WorkFlowName <string>
        Name of workflow to apply access control to.
        
        Required?                    false
        Position?                    named
        Default value                None
        Accept pipeline input?       False
        Accept wildcard characters?  false
        
    -StateName <string>
        Name of workflow state to apply access control to.
        
        Required?                    false
        Position?                    named
        Default value                None
        Accept pipeline input?       False
        Accept wildcard characters?  false
        
    -IncludeInheritance [<SwitchParameter>]
        When included, if access control is being inherited, it will be 
        added directly to the folder or document security.
        
        Required?                    false
        Position?                    named
        Default value                False
        Accept pipeline input?       False
        Accept wildcard characters?  false
        
    <CommonParameters>
        This cmdlet supports the common parameters: Verbose, Debug,
        ErrorAction, ErrorVariable, WarningAction, WarningVariable,
        OutBuffer, PipelineVariable, and OutVariable. For more information, 
    see 
        about_CommonParameters 
    (https:/go.microsoft.com/fwlink/?LinkID=113216). 
    
INPUTS
    PWPS_DAB.CommonTypes+ProjectWiseFolder[]
        The InputFolders parameter contains the folder(s) to apply the 
        access control to.
    
    
OUTPUTS
    
NOTES
    
    
        
    
    -------------------------- EXAMPLE 1 --------------------------
    
    PS C:\>Get-PWFolders -FolderPath 'Projects' -Slow | 
    Update-PWFolderSecurity -MemberType ʺuʺ -MemberName "username" 
    -MemberAccess "c", "r", "w", "d" -FolderSecurity -Verbose
    
    
    -------------------------- EXAMPLE 2 --------------------------
    
    PS C:\>Get-PWFolders -FolderPath 'Projects' -JustOne -Slow |  
    Update-PWFolderSecurity -MemberType ʺgʺ -MemberName "New Group" 
    -MemberAccess "r", "w", "fr", "fw" -FolderSecurity -DocumentSecurity 
    -Verbose
    
    
    -------------------------- EXAMPLE 3 --------------------------
    
    PS C:\>Get-PWFolders -FolderPath 'Projects' -Slow |  
    Update-PWFolderSecurity -MemberType ʺulʺ -MemberName "All_Users" 
    -MemberAccess "r", "fr" -DocumentSecurity -Verbose
    
    
    -------------------------- EXAMPLE 4 --------------------------
    
    PS C:\>Get-PWFolders -FolderPath 'Projects' -Slow |  
    Update-PWFolderSecurity -MemberType ʺulʺ -MemberName "All_Users" -Role 
    "Project Observer" -Verbose
    
    
    -------------------------- EXAMPLE 5 --------------------------
    
    PS C:\>Get-PWFolders -FolderPath 'Projects' -JustOne -Slow | 
    Update-PWFolderSecurity -MemberType ʺulʺ -MemberName "All_Users" -Role 
    "Project Observer" -IncludeInheritance -Verbose
    
    
    -------------------------- EXAMPLE 6 --------------------------
    
    PS C:\>Get-PWFolders -FolderPath 'Projects' -JustOne -Slow | 
    Update-PWFolderSecurity -MemberType ʺEveryoneʺ -Role "Project Observer" 
    -IncludeInheritance -Verbose
    
    
    -------------------------- EXAMPLE 7 --------------------------
    
    PS C:\>Get-PWFolders -FolderPath 'Projects' -JustOne -Slow | 
    Update-PWFolderSecurity -MemberType 'UserList' -MemberAccessString 
    '--CDrw-RWf-' -FolderSecurity -DocumentSecurity -IncludeInheritance 
    -Verbose
    
    
    
RELATED LINKS

## Update-PWGroup


NAME
    Update-PWGroup
    
SYNOPSIS
    Modifies a Group in the current ProjectWise Datasource.
    
    
SYNTAX
    Update-PWGroup [-Group] <Group> [<CommonParameters>]
    
    
DESCRIPTION
    The Update-PWGroup cmdlet modifies the properties of a Group object in 
    the current ProjectWise Datasource.
    
    Update-PWGroup takes a Group object that has had one or more properties 
    modified and updates the corresponding properties of the Group with the 
    same GroupID in the Datasource.
    
    The following properties can be modified on the Group object before 
    passing it to Update-PWGroup: GroupName, Description, SecProvider, Type, 
    and IsMembershipUpdatedBySTS.
    

PARAMETERS
    -Group <Group>
        The Group to update in the Datasource. This object must have had one 
        or more of the following properties modified: GroupName, 
        Description, SecProvider, Type, or IsMembershipUpdatedBySTS.
        
        Required?                    true
        Position?                    0
        Default value                None
        Accept pipeline input?       True (ByValue)
        Accept wildcard characters?  false
        
    <CommonParameters>
        This cmdlet supports the common parameters: Verbose, Debug,
        ErrorAction, ErrorVariable, WarningAction, WarningVariable,
        OutBuffer, PipelineVariable, and OutVariable. For more information, 
    see 
        about_CommonParameters 
    (https:/go.microsoft.com/fwlink/?LinkID=113216). 
    
INPUTS
    Bentley.ProjectWise.PowerShell.Common.Group
        You can pipe modified Group objects to Update-PWGroup to update that 
        Group.
    
    
OUTPUTS
    Bentley.ProjectWise.PowerShell.Common.Group
        Update-PWGroup returns an object that represents the modified Group.
    
    
NOTES
    
    
        Update-PWGroup requires an open ProjectWise Connection in the 
        current thread.
    
    -------------------------- EXAMPLE 1 --------------------------
    
    $groupObj = Get-PWGroup -GroupID 3
    $groupObj.GroupName = "DifferentGroupName"
    $groupObj.Description = "Some New Description"
    $groupObj.Type = "Logical"
    $groupObj.SecProvider = "NEWDOMAIN"
    Update-PWGroup -Group $groupObj
    
    Changes the group's name, description, type, and security provider. The 
    type must be either "Windows" or "Logical".
    -------------------------- EXAMPLE 2 --------------------------
    
    $groupObj = Get-PWGroup -GroupID 3
    $groupObj.IsMembershipUpdatedBySTS = $true
    Update-PWGroup -Group $groupObj
    
    Enables IMS/STS Claims membership update for the group by setting the 
    IsMembershipUpdatedBySTS property to $true on the Group object before 
    passing it to Update-PWGroup.
    -------------------------- EXAMPLE 3 --------------------------
    
    $groupObj = Get-PWGroup -GroupID 3
    $groupObj.IsMembershipUpdatedBySTS = $false
    Update-PWGroup -Group $groupObj
    
    Disables IMS/STS Claims membership update for the group by setting the 
    IsMembershipUpdatedBySTS property to $false on the Group object before 
    passing it to Update-PWGroup.
    
RELATED LINKS





--

## Update-PWIndexServer


NAME
    Update-PWIndexServer
    
SYNOPSIS
    Updates the provided Index Server in the current ProjectWise Datasource.
    
    
SYNTAX
    Update-PWIndexServer [[-Server] <IndexServer>] [<CommonParameters>]
    
    
DESCRIPTION
    The Update-PWIndexServer cmdlet updates the provided Index Server in the 
    active ProjectWise Datasource.
    

PARAMETERS
    -Server <IndexServer>
        The Index Server object to update.
        
        Required?                    false
        Position?                    0
        Default value                None
        Accept pipeline input?       True (ByValue)
        Accept wildcard characters?  false
        
    <CommonParameters>
        This cmdlet supports the common parameters: Verbose, Debug,
        ErrorAction, ErrorVariable, WarningAction, WarningVariable,
        OutBuffer, PipelineVariable, and OutVariable. For more information, 
    see 
        about_CommonParameters 
    (https:/go.microsoft.com/fwlink/?LinkID=113216). 
    
INPUTS
    
OUTPUTS
    
NOTES
    
    
        This cmdlet requires an open administrative ProjectWise Connection 
        in the current thread. You can open an administrative Connection 
        using the Open-PWConnection cmdlet and setting the -Admin flag.
    
    -------------------------- Example 1 --------------------------
    
    $server = (Get-PWIndexServer)[1]
    $server.Name = "SomeOtherName"
    Update-PWIndexServer -Server $server
    
    Update the name of the second registered Index Server.
    
    Get-PWIndexServer
    
    Name     HostName      ServiceUrl Guid
    ----     --------      ---------- ----
    <local>  localhost                00000000-0000-0000-0000-000000000000 
    SomeName 169.254.31.62            4bd35d86-ef3e-4b61-b7b3-729278547e5d
    
    $server = (Get-PWIndexServer)[1] $server.Name = "SomeOtherName" 
    Update-PWIndexServer -Server $server
    
    Get-PWIndexServer
    
    Name          HostName      ServiceUrl Guid
    ----          --------      ---------- ----
    <local>       localhost                
    00000000-0000-0000-0000-000000000000 SomeOtherName 169.254.31.62         
       4bd35d86-ef3e-4b61-b7b3-729278547e5d

## Update-PWUser


NAME
    Update-PWUser
    
SYNOPSIS
    Modifies a User in the current ProjectWise Datasource.
    
    
SYNTAX
    Update-PWUser [-User] <User> [[-Password] <Object>] 
    [-ClearIdentityProvider] [<CommonParameters>]
    
    
DESCRIPTION
    The Update-PWUser cmdlet modifies the properties of a User object in the 
    current ProjectWise Datasource.
    
    Update-PWUser takes a User object that has had one or more properties 
    modified and updates the corresponding properties of the User with the 
    same UserID in the Datasource. With the Password parameter, the cmdlet 
    can also change the password for a specific User. Password can be passed 
    as secure string (recommended) or a plain text.
    

PARAMETERS
    -ClearIdentityProvider [<SwitchParameter>]
        If set, clears associated identity provider data.
        
        Required?                    false
        Position?                    named
        Default value                False
        Accept pipeline input?       False
        Accept wildcard characters?  false
        
    -Password <Object>
        The new password to set for this User. Password can be passed as 
        plain text (String) or SecureString object. It is recommended to use 
        SecureString (see examples).
        
        Required?                    false
        Position?                    1
        Default value                None
        Accept pipeline input?       False
        Accept wildcard characters?  false
        
    -User <User>
        The User to update in the Datasource. Unless the Password parameter 
        is specified, this object must have had one or more of the following 
        properties modified: UserName, Description, Email, SecProvider, 
        Type, or Identity.
        
        Required?                    true
        Position?                    0
        Default value                None
        Accept pipeline input?       True (ByValue)
        Accept wildcard characters?  false
        
    <CommonParameters>
        This cmdlet supports the common parameters: Verbose, Debug,
        ErrorAction, ErrorVariable, WarningAction, WarningVariable,
        OutBuffer, PipelineVariable, and OutVariable. For more information, 
    see 
        about_CommonParameters 
    (https:/go.microsoft.com/fwlink/?LinkID=113216). 
    
INPUTS
    Bentley.ProjectWise.PowerShell.Common.User
        You can pipe modified User objects to Update-PWUser to update that 
        User.
    
    
OUTPUTS
    Bentley.ProjectWise.PowerShell.Common.User
        Update-PWUser returns an object that represents the modified User.
    
    
NOTES
    
    
        Currently, changing a User's Type from Windows to WinSync (or 
        vice-versa) will have no effect. Update-PWUser requires an open 
        ProjectWise Connection in the current thread.  You can open a 
        Connection with Open-PWConnection before calling Update-PWUser. 
        Update-PWUser requires an Admin session.  To open an admin session, 
        use the -Admin flag when opening the ProjectWise Connection with 
        Open-PWConnection. It is recommended to store and pass password as 
        secure string object.
    
    -------------------------- EXAMPLE 1 --------------------------
    
    $userObj = Get-PWUser -UserID 4
    $userObj.UserName = "Changed.Name"
    $userObj.Description = "Some New Description"
    $userObj.Email = "changed@email.com"
    $userObj.Type = "Windows"
    $userObj.SecProvider = "NEWDOMAIN"
    $userObj.Identity = "external_identity@email.com"
    Update-PWUser -User $userObj | Format-List
    
    Modifies various user properties.
    
    UserID           : 4 UserName         : Changed.Name Description      : 
    Some New Description Email            : changed@email.com SecProvider    
      : NEWDOMAIN Type             : Windows IsConnected      : False 
    Disabled         : True CreationDate     : 2012.12.24 17:22:19 LastLogin 
           : Identity         : external_identity@email.com IdentityProvider 
    : ID               : 4 Name             : Changed.Name
    -------------------------- EXAMPLE 2 --------------------------
    
    Get-PWUser -UserID 1 | Update-PWUser -Password N3wPassw0rd
    
    Modifies user password.
    
    UserID UserName      Description           Type     SecProvider  
    IsConnected IsDisabled ------ --------      -----------           ----   
      -----------  ----------- ----------   1    admin         An admin user 
            Logical                    Y           N
    -------------------------- EXAMPLE 3 --------------------------
    
    $SecurePassword = Read-Host -Prompt "Enter password" -AsSecureString 
    Update-PWUser -User $userObj -Password $SecurePassword
    
    Modifies user password using secure string entered in the console.
    
    UserID UserName      Description           Type     SecProvider  
    IsConnected IsDisabled ------ --------      -----------           ----   
      -----------  ----------- ----------   1    admin         An admin user 
            Logical                    Y           N
    
RELATED LINKS
    Get-PWUser 
    New-PWUser 
    Remove-PWUser

## Update-PWUserList


NAME
    Update-PWUserList
    
SYNOPSIS
    Modifies a User List in the current ProjectWise Datasource.
    
    
SYNTAX
    Update-PWUserList [-UserList] <UserList> [<CommonParameters>]
    
    
DESCRIPTION
    The Update-PWUserList cmdlet modifies the properties of a UserList 
    object in the current ProjectWise Datasource.
    
    Update-PWUserList takes a UserList object that has had one or more 
    properties modified and updates the corresponding properties of the 
    UserList with the same UserListID in the Datasource.
    

PARAMETERS
    -UserList <UserList>
        The UserList to update in the Datasource. This object must have had 
        one or more of the following properties modified: UserListName or 
        Description
        
        Required?                    true
        Position?                    0
        Default value                None
        Accept pipeline input?       True (ByValue)
        Accept wildcard characters?  false
        
    <CommonParameters>
        This cmdlet supports the common parameters: Verbose, Debug,
        ErrorAction, ErrorVariable, WarningAction, WarningVariable,
        OutBuffer, PipelineVariable, and OutVariable. For more information, 
    see 
        about_CommonParameters 
    (https:/go.microsoft.com/fwlink/?LinkID=113216).

    Bentley.ProjectWise.PowerShell.Common.UserList

        update that UserList.
    
    
OUTPUTS

        Update-PWUserList returns an object that represents the modified 
        UserList.

        Update-PWUserList requires an open ProjectWise Connection in the 
        current thread.  You can open a Connection with Open-PWConnection 
        before calling Update-PWUserList.
        
        Update-PWUserList requires an Admin session.  To open an admin

## Update-PWUserNativeIdentity


NAME
    Update-PWUserNativeIdentity
    
SYNOPSIS
    Changes current user password in the current ProjectWise Datasource.
    
    
SYNTAX
    Update-PWUserNativeIdentity [-UserID] <Int32> [-Password] <Object> 
    [<CommonParameters>]
    
    Update-PWUserNativeIdentity [-UserName] <String> [-Password] <Object> 
    [<CommonParameters>]
    
    
DESCRIPTION
    The Update-PWUserNativeIdentity cmdlet changes password currently 
    assigned to a User in the current ProjectWise Datasource.
    
    User can be selected either by ID or by Name.
    

PARAMETERS
    -Password <Object>
        The password value to assign.
        
        Required?                    true
        Position?                    1
        Default value                None
        Accept pipeline input?       True (ByPropertyName)
        Accept wildcard characters?  false
        
    -UserID <Int32>
        The numerical identifier of the User to change password for.
        
        Required?                    true
        Position?                    0
        Default value                None
        Accept pipeline input?       True (ByPropertyName)
        Accept wildcard characters?  false
        
    -UserName <String>
        The textual identifier of the User to change password for.
        
        Required?                    true
        Position?                    0
        Default value                None
        Accept pipeline input?       True (ByPropertyName)
        Accept wildcard characters?  false
        
    <CommonParameters>
        This cmdlet supports the common parameters: Verbose, Debug,
        ErrorAction, ErrorVariable, WarningAction, WarningVariable,
        OutBuffer, PipelineVariable, and OutVariable. For more information, 
    see 
        about_CommonParameters 
    (https:/go.microsoft.com/fwlink/?LinkID=113216). 
    
INPUTS
    
OUTPUTS
    Bentley.ProjectWise.PowerShell.Common.User
        Update-PWUserIdentity returns an object that represents the User for 
        which password was changed.
    
    
NOTES
    
    
        Update-PWUserNativeIdentity requires an open ProjectWise Connection 
        in the current thread.  You can open a Connection with 
        Open-PWConnection before calling Update-PWUserNativeIdentity.
        
        Update-PWUserNativeIdentity requires an Admin session.  To open an 
        admin session, use the -Admin flag when opening the ProjectWise 
        Connection with Open-PWConnection.
    
    --------------------------- EXAMPLE1 ---------------------------
    
    Update-PWUserNativeIdentity -UserName "user" -Password  
    (ConvertTo-SecureString "password" -AsPlainText -Force)
    
    Changes password of ProjectWise user named "user". This password then 
    can be used to perform Native login to PWDI server.

## Update-PWWorkAreaProperties


NAME
    Update-PWWorkAreaProperties
    
SYNOPSIS
    Updates the properties of individual Work Areas.
    
    
SYNTAX
    Update-PWWorkAreaProperties [[-InputFolder] <ProjectWiseFolder[]>] 
    [[-FolderPath] <String>] [[-ProjectProperties] <Hashtable>] 
    [[-LookupPropertiesToSet] <String[]>] [[-DisablePicklistPropcessing]] 
    [<CommonParameters>]
    
    
DESCRIPTION
    The `Update-PWWorkAreaProperties` cmdlet updates the property values of 
    Work Areas in ProjectWise.
    
    You can specify a Work Area by:
    
    - Folder path : Use the `-FolderPath` parameter to update a single Work 
    Area by its path. - Pipeline input : Pipe Work Area objects from 
    `Get-PWWorkAreas` to update multiple Work Areas.
    
    Properties are specified as a hashtable of property names and values 
    using the `-ProjectProperties` parameter. Property names are 
    case-sensitive.
    
    The cmdlet automatically processes picklist properties. For 
    cascading/dependent picklist properties (where one property's value 
    filters the available values of another), use the 
    `-LookupPropertiesToSet` parameter to specify the order in which 
    properties should be processed.
    
    Use `Get-PWWorkAreaProperties` to discover available property names 
    before updating.
    
    The cmdlet returns the updated Work Area objects with project properties 
    populated.
    

PARAMETERS
    -InputFolder <ProjectWiseFolder[]>
        Work Area folder objects from pipeline. Use `Get-PWWorkAreas` to 
        retrieve Work Areas and pipe them to this cmdlet.
        
        Required?                    false
        Position?                    0
        Default value                None
        Accept pipeline input?       True (ByValue)
        Accept wildcard characters?  false
        
    -FolderPath <String>
        Full folder path of the Work Area to update. Use this parameter to 
        update a single Work Area by its path.
        
        Required?                    false
        Position?                    1
        Default value                None
        Accept pipeline input?       False
        Accept wildcard characters?  false
        
    -ProjectProperties <Hashtable>
        Hashtable containing property names and values to update. Property 
        names are case-sensitive.
        
        EXAMPLE: `@{PROJECT_Project_Name = "My Project"; 
        PROJECT_Project_Number = "PRJ-001"}`
        
        Required?                    false
        Position?                    2
        Default value                None
        Accept pipeline input?       False
        Accept wildcard characters?  false
        
    -LookupPropertiesToSet <String[]>
        Array of picklist property names to set in a specific order for 
        cascading/dependent picklists. Properties are processed in the order 
        specified, allowing dependent picklists to filter based on 
        previously set values. If a filtered picklist resolves to exactly 
        one value, it is automatically set.
        
        EXAMPLE: `@("PROJECT_Country", "PROJECT_State", "PROJECT_City")`
        
        Required?                    false
        Position?                    3
        Default value                None
        Accept pipeline input?       False
        Accept wildcard characters?  false
        
    -DisablePicklistPropcessing [<SwitchParameter>]
        Disables automatic picklist property processing. Use this switch if 
        picklist properties are being set to unexpected values.
        
        Required?                    false
        Position?                    4
        Default value                False
        Accept pipeline input?       False
        Accept wildcard characters?  false
        
    <CommonParameters>
        This cmdlet supports the common parameters: Verbose, Debug,
        ErrorAction, ErrorVariable, WarningAction, WarningVariable,
        OutBuffer, PipelineVariable, and OutVariable. For more information, 
    see 
        about_CommonParameters 
    (https:/go.microsoft.com/fwlink/?LinkID=113216). 
    
INPUTS
    PWPS_DAB.CommonTypes.ProjectWiseFolder[]
        You can pipe Work Area folder objects from `Get-PWWorkAreas` to this 
        cmdlet.
    
    
OUTPUTS
    PWPS_DAB.CommonTypes.ProjectWiseFolder[]
        Returns an array of updated ProjectWiseFolder objects with project 
        properties populated. The returned objects include all standard Work 
        Area properties plus the updated custom properties.
    
    
NOTES
    
    
        - This cmdlet requires an active connection to a ProjectWise 
        datasource.
        
        - Property names are case-sensitive.
        
        - Date values should be provided as strings in the format 
        "yyyy-MM-dd HH:mm:ss".
        
        - The cmdlet automatically processes picklist properties unless 
        `-DisablePicklistPropcessing` is specified.
        
        - For cascading/dependent picklists, use `-LookupPropertiesToSet` to 
        specify the order of property processing.
        
        - If a filtered picklist resolves to exactly one value, it is 
        automatically set. If it resolves to multiple values, a warning is 
        displayed.
        
        - Use `Get-PWWorkAreaProperties` to discover available property 
        names before updating.
        
        - Only Work Area folders (not regular folders) can be updated with 
        this cmdlet.
    
    ------ EXAMPLE 1: Update a single property by folder path ------
    
    Update-PWWorkAreaProperties -FolderPath 
    "Projects\Engineering\Project001" -ProjectProperties 
    @{PROJECT_Project_Name = "New Project Name"}
    
    Updates the `PROJECT_Project_Name` property of the Work Area at the 
    specified folder path.
    ----- EXAMPLE 2: Update multiple properties by folder path -----
    
    Update-PWWorkAreaProperties -FolderPath 
    "Projects\Engineering\Project001" -ProjectProperties 
    @{PROJECT_Project_Name = "My Project"; PROJECT_Project_Number = 
    "PRJ-001"; PROJECT_Project_Manager_Email = "john.smith@EXAMPLE.com"}
    
    Updates multiple properties of a Work Area at the specified folder path.
    ------ EXAMPLE 3: Update Work Areas using pipeline input ------
    
    Get-PWWorkAreas -FolderPath "Projects\Engineering" | 
    Update-PWWorkAreaProperties -ProjectProperties @{PROJECT_Status = 
    "Active"}
    
    Retrieves all Work Areas within the "Projects\Engineering" folder and 
    updates the `PROJECT_Status` property on each one.
    -------------- EXAMPLE 4: Update a date property --------------
    
    Update-PWWorkAreaProperties -FolderPath 
    "Projects\Engineering\Project001" -ProjectProperties 
    @{PROJECT_Start_Date = "2024-01-15 09:00:00"}
    
    Updates a date property of a Work Area. Date values should be provided 
    as strings in the format "yyyy-MM-dd HH:mm:ss".
    ---------- EXAMPLE 5: Update multiple date properties ----------
    
    Update-PWWorkAreaProperties -FolderPath 
    "Projects\Engineering\Project001" -ProjectProperties 
    @{PROJECT_Start_Date = "2024-01-15 09:00:00"; PROJECT_End_Date = 
    "2024-12-31 17:00:00"}
    
    Updates multiple date properties of a Work Area.
    ------------- EXAMPLE 6: Update a numeric property -------------
    
    Update-PWWorkAreaProperties -FolderPath 
    "Projects\Engineering\Project001" -ProjectProperties @{PROJECT_Budget = 
    50000}
    
    Updates a numeric property of a Work Area.
    ------- EXAMPLE 7: Update cascading picklist properties -------
    
    Update-PWWorkAreaProperties -FolderPath 
    "Projects\Engineering\Project001" -ProjectProperties @{PROJECT_Country = 
    "USA"} -LookupPropertiesToSet @("PROJECT_Country", "PROJECT_State", 
    "PROJECT_City")
    
    Updates cascading picklist properties in the specified order. The 
    `PROJECT_Country` is set first, which filters the available 
    `PROJECT_State` values, which then filters the available `PROJECT_City` 
    values. Properties are auto-populated if only one value is available in 
    the filtered picklist.
    ----- EXAMPLE 8: Update with picklist processing disabled -----
    
    Update-PWWorkAreaProperties -FolderPath 
    "Projects\Engineering\Project001" -ProjectProperties 
    @{PROJECT_Project_Name = "My Project"} -DisablePicklistPropcessing
    
    Updates properties without automatic picklist processing. Use this 
    switch if picklist properties are being set to unexpected values.
    --- EXAMPLE 9: Update Work Areas of a specific project type ---
    
    Get-PWWorkAreas -ProjectTypeName "General" -PopulateProjectProperties | 
    Update-PWWorkAreaProperties -ProjectProperties @{PROJECT_Status = 
    "Active"}
    
    Retrieves all Work Areas of the "General" project type and updates the 
    `PROJECT_Status` property on each one.
    EXAMPLE 10: Update Work Areas matching specific property criteria
    
    Get-PWWorkAreas -ProjectTypeName "General" -ProjectProperties 
    @{PROJECT_Status = "Pending"} -PopulateProjectProperties | 
    Update-PWWorkAreaProperties -ProjectProperties @{PROJECT_Status = 
    "Active"}
    
    Retrieves all Work Areas with `PROJECT_Status` set to "Pending" and 
    updates them to "Active".
    ---- EXAMPLE 11: Discover properties then update Work Area ----
    
    Get-PWWorkAreaProperties -ProjectType "General"
    Update-PWWorkAreaProperties -FolderPath 
    "Projects\Engineering\Project001" -ProjectProperties 
    @{PROJECT_Project_Name = "My Project"}
    
    First discovers available properties for the "General" project type 
    using `Get-PWWorkAreaProperties`, then updates a Work Area using one of 
    those properties.
    EXAMPLE 12: Update Work Areas within a folder path with specific project 
    type
    
    Get-PWWorkAreas -FolderPath "Projects\Engineering" -ProjectTypeName 
    "General" -PopulateProjectProperties | Update-PWWorkAreaProperties 
    -ProjectProperties @{PROJECT_Priority = "High"}
    
    Retrieves all Work Areas of the "General" project type within the 
    "Projects\Engineering" folder and updates the `PROJECT_Priority` 
    property.
    ---------- EXAMPLE 13: Update only connected projects ----------
    
    Get-PWWorkAreas -OnlyConnectedProjects -PopulateProjectProperties | 
    Update-PWWorkAreaProperties -ProjectProperties 
    @{PROJECT_Connection_Status = "Verified"}
    
    Retrieves only connected Work Areas and updates a property on each one.
    ------ EXAMPLE 14: Store updated Work Areas in a variable ------
    
    $updatedWorkAreas = Update-PWWorkAreaProperties -FolderPath 
    "Projects\Engineering\Project001" -ProjectProperties 
    @{PROJECT_Project_Name = "Updated Project"}
    $updatedWorkAreas
    
    Updates a Work Area and stores the returned updated Work Area object in 
    a variable for further use.
    ------ EXAMPLE 15: Update Work Areas with verbose output ------
    
    Update-PWWorkAreaProperties -FolderPath 
    "Projects\Engineering\Project001" -ProjectProperties @{PROJECT_Country = 
    "USA"} -LookupPropertiesToSet @("PROJECT_Country", "PROJECT_State") 
    -Verbose
    
    Updates cascading picklist properties with verbose output showing the 
    values being set for each property.
    
RELATED LINKS





--
